# Changelog

One row per version of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. A structural change bumps the
`version:` there and adds its row here in the same diff.

| ver | date | change |
|---|---|---|
| 0.290.0 | 2026-09-22 | The five failure classes the paid G2 confirmation found are removed at their decision rather than reworded (D227, extends D223 and D225; Closes #487). This is stage G2b of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. Declaration: the new `plan/declare.rs::normalize` runs before the parse on the plan tool and on `plan.op`. A `cmd` item's checker written as the shell command it is becomes a checker manifest the host builds (`snapshot_root`, no protected paths, 60 s like the library's `cmd()`) and names by digest; a worktree delegation with no `contract` whose `accept` is `{command}` gets that command as its one critical `writer` item, so D223's rule still holds; an `isolation` beside `spec` moves into it (a different second value is refused), `delegate` reads as `delegation` and a todo-level `accept` as the delegation's. The blobs land in the plan's store after the op commits and before the scheduling pass (`PlanEngine::apply_with`). In the library `Writer(accept="make -s check")` is one critical command. Waiting: `rlm.wait` with no cursor uses the caller's last one, so it blocks until a child moves; `LoopConfig::waiting`, the todo coupling's live-children read, exempts a repeated batch from the repeat breaker while a child is live or its finish is settling (`SubagentHost::busy` counts finishes in flight); `yi ask`, now `crates/cli/src/ask.rs`, holds the run open at an `AgentEnd` while the host is busy and ends once it is idle and no turn followed. Lanes: the engine opens its lane pool at the first checkout that needs one (`with_lane_home`), so a repository initialised mid-session gets its candidates verified; a candidate commit leaves `__pycache__`, `*.pyc` and `.pytest_cache` out and commits nothing when that is all there is; `evals/surface.py` seeds its workspace as a git repository. Owner steps: an owner `start` on a delegated todo, and a `submit` or `done` on one when the finish hook is installed (`Delegate::finishes`), answers `nothing to do: the engine starts delegated todos; "alpha" is running by <agent>; ...` and journals nothing, unless the plan is not active or a spawn intent is pending; the `plan-fanout` and `plan-child-submit` scenarios and the directed instructions no longer tell the owner to step, and `fan-out` gains a directed instruction and a git repository. Briefs: a delegation note over 1024 bytes is stored whole as `text/markdown` in the plan's store, the note keeps its head and a `note_ref`, and the brief says `read plan://<plan>/artifacts/<hex>`; the library stores the same bytes itself, so a redeclaration is no drift; `read` resolves any `scheme://` address through the session's resolver (`fetch/read.rs::route_urls`). Also: `view` shows the plan written last when none is open; the phase refusal reads `it needs a candidate_submitted record on this attempt`; `history://<plan>/<todo>/tail/1` finds the agent by the longest prefix; an op waits out a short lease hold instead of refusing. growth +4890: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +578 (the stack of twenty-five stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D227, stands at +17103 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: thirty-one comment lines of this change to keep the comment volume at 3600; weighed and kept: `yi ask`'s poll while held, since a steer that lands between two runs starts none. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.289.0 | 2026-09-22 | With a plan open the session todo list is the plan's view: there is one list, and the todo tool no longer steps it (D226, amends D137; Closes #486). This is stage G3 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. The new `todo/mirror.rs::Mirror` wraps the depth-0 engine's `OpSink` (the `TodoStore` is now built in `wire_plan_request`, before the engine): after the inner sink records an op it reads the op's root plan and hands `TodoStore::replace` the projection, one phase named by the plan id and one item per todo with its children, the todo's state, a blocked note or a fail cause as `note`, `extra.plan`, and `extra.by` while it runs; an item keeps the `t{n}` id its label already had and a new label takes the next. `replace` records a `custom{todo}` entry with op `plan` and actor `engine` and runs the change hooks, so the environment block's `todos:` line, ACP's `_yi/todo`, the TUI and console HUD and `yi todo` show the plan with no reader changed; an unchanged projection records nothing. While the plan is active the list carries `extra.plan`, and `TodoStore::apply_as` refuses every state-changing op on it with `TodoError::Mirrored`: "the list is plan {plan}; the plan tool changes it (append, block, drop, start and done on your own todos; the engine steps delegated todos)", one guard for the tool, the seed prelude and ACP; `view` passes. The todo tool does not forward to plan ops: a second road to one op teaches two. A mirrored list offers no `next:` todo moves, a failed item renders `(failed: <cause>)`, and the todo coupling defers to the plan coupling on it: no prelude or seed, no work nudge or closed-list nudge, and `stop_posture` reads it as quiet, so a stop over running delegated children is the plan coupling's to judge. `loop_coupling.rs::reinjection_text` keeps `frontier_text`, since the todo header carries counts and one running label, not the ready todos with their checks. The plan tool description's "the todo tool is the list" becomes "the todo list shows it", and doctrine's todo, planning and flow passages say an open plan is the list and drop "your todo blocked `on child`"; the request budget falls 47754 to 47688 bytes. The `plan-fanout` surface scenario gains `noRefusalsFrom: ["todo"]`. The D224 row names `dispatch_ready_in`, the probe backstop's name since 0.288.0. The G2 review's findings are folded in, each with a test seen red first: `Run.settle` gives a finished child whose todo the engine left running one wait round and then collects it, and `Run.launch` does not adopt that attempt again, so a run with no budget returns `unresolved` instead of waiting forever; the finish hook runs an uncontracted delegation's accept command (the child's `check`) before the engine's `done` and routes the discoveries of a `{value, discoveries}` answer as `rlm.result` does, and a red check fails the todo with `its check is red: <evidence>`; `done.rs::admit` reads the engine carve-out off `Txn::engine`, set from `Actor::Engine`, so a child named `engine` cannot submit for another child's todo; the start-refusal cache keys a contract refusal on which criteria are stored, so storing a missing checker lets the next op start the todo; a finish the engine could not accept re-reads the todo, says `plan: "alpha" waits on you: <note>` when the verdict blocked it on the user and nothing when an owner op won the race; the hook reads only the root a `<plan>/<todo>` child names and returns at once for any other name. Tests: `todo_mirror::a_plan_op_replaces_the_list_and_keeps_ids`, `a_mirrored_item_refuses_start_with_the_plan_road`, `a_mirrored_list_with_a_running_child_does_not_nag` (T0) and `a_plan_opened_through_the_tool_is_the_sessions_todo_list` (T1, wiring and the environment's header line); `plan_ops::a_child_named_engine_is_not_the_engine`, `contracts::a_start_refused_for_a_missing_criterion_is_tried_once_it_is_stored`, `contracts::an_escalated_finish_tells_the_owner_the_todo_waits_on_them` and `plan::dispatch::a_red_accept_command_fails_the_finish` (T0, new, the G2 findings); `test_yi_plan::test_a_verdict_that_judges_no_product_leaves_the_attempt_alone` runs without a budget under a five-second bound. Existing todo tests are unchanged. Not built: the `locate` read-cost fix has no test, since its contract is cost; the TUI HUD still shows the plan line above the mirrored list; the `plan-fanout` confirmation, which is paid and user-run. growth +4312: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +252 (the stack of twenty-four stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D226, stands at +16525 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: doctrine's blocked-on-child line, four restating comments; weighed and kept: `frontier_text` in the re-injection. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.288.0 | 2026-09-22 | A plan child's finish is its submission and its acceptance: no owner op is needed to submit, verify, accept, refuse or fail a delegated todo whose child ended (D225, extends D222; Closes #485). This is stage G2 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. `SubagentHost` gains a late-bound `FinishFn` (`set_finished`, the `lease.rs` pattern, since the host is built before the engine), installed by `wire_plan_engine` at depth 0 and holding host and engine weakly; `conclude` consults it after `publish` and the bill for a completed child that is neither asking (D165) nor a service and for a failed or interrupted one, and a child it takes sends no lifecycle notice. The new `plan/finish.rs::finish_child` finds the todo `Running` by that child in an active plan (none means an `rlm.run` child, and conclude's own notice runs), stores the child's last answer in the plan's artifact store as `plan://<id>/artifacts/<digest>` (`application/json` when a declared schema or a schema item reads it), applies `submit` as `Actor::Engine` unless the child submitted for this attempt itself (the phase for a worktree todo, the submitted url for an inline one), then `done`, which for a worktree todo accepts the verified integration and reaps the child, so the promoted transcript and the `history://` pin still reach the owner. A `fail` verdict applies `fail` with cause `contract refused: <items>` and disposition `retained`; an abstention, an escalation or a merge conflict leaves the todo running for the owner's `fail`, `retry` or the user's acceptance; a failed or interrupted child's todo is failed with its error. The owner is told once over the `plan_relevance` Steer that wakes an idle owner: `plan: accepted "alpha" (agent://...)`, `plan: refused "alpha": <item>: <detail>` or `plan: failed "alpha": <cause>`. `done.rs::admit` lets the engine submit for the running child. The child brief loses both submit lines, doctrine's plan flow says the engine starts, submits and accepts or refuses (the request budget stays at 47754 bytes), and `help(yi)`'s Todo line says the engine steps a delegated todo. In the library `Run.settle` reads a delegated todo's state and sends no `done` (a finished child whose todo is still running is the engine's or waits on the owner), `Run._complete` is the inline road alone, and `Todo.cancel` fails a running delegated todo without an interrupt, since the interrupt handed its end to the engine first; the fake host runs the same finish at the wait that reports the child. The G1 review's findings are folded in: the engine's start-refusal cache keeps only a contract refusal, keyed by the contract digest, and a fuse refusal, keyed by the spawn count, so a transient refusal (a full host, a spawn that failed) is tried again by the next op; an engine start another pass got to first (`illegal_step`, `admission`, `unmet_edge`, `needs_reconciliation`) journals and says nothing; the probe backstop, `dispatch_ready_in`, walks only the roots this session's own ledger names; `view` returns the held todos and the engine's start refusal on the current attempt, `plan.op` replies carry `held`, and `Run.launch` answers `admission` only for a held todo and otherwise surfaces the engine's refusal as `not_started`; the review pod holds its declared arbiter on a reader instead of the user; the in-order adopt loop's missing refusal guard is moot, since the settle no longer sends `done` for a delegated todo, and the guard would strand a todo the engine starts after a refusal. Not built: `stale_reminder` stays, since a ready inline todo can still go unclaimed and the reminder counts it; the plan's re-drive of a finished but unsubmitted child after a crash is the existing repair road. Tests: `plan::dispatch::an_inline_childs_finish_is_accepted_without_an_owner_op`, `an_asking_child_is_not_submitted`, `an_rlm_run_child_is_not_a_plan_finish`, `a_brief_never_names_submit` (T0, new) and `a_failed_childs_last_product_survives_the_reap` (rewritten: the engine fails the todo from the child's error); `lanes::accept::a_finished_worktree_child_is_accepted_without_an_owner_op`, `an_early_submit_is_accepted_at_the_finish_and_not_submitted_again` and `a_red_contract_fails_the_todo_retained` (T1, new); `plan_ops::a_transient_spawn_failure_is_tried_again_by_the_next_op`, `a_raced_engine_start_journals_nothing` and `a_view_names_the_todos_the_width_holds` and `plan_probe::the_backstop_starts_only_the_roots_this_session_owns` (T0, new, the G1 findings); `test_yi_plan::test_a_red_verdict_fails_the_attempt_through_the_engine_and_a_stuck_child_is_waited_on` (moved to the engine path), `test_an_engine_refusal_is_not_read_as_a_wait_for_a_slot` (new), the abstain and cancel tests and `test_yi_shapes`' pod and overhead counts (fork_join 9 requests over 2 by hand, scatter 16 over 2) rewritten to pin the engine's finish; `a_worktree_brief_names_the_submit_verb` is deleted with the lines it pinned; the `kernel_data_surface` T2 rigs hand each child's end to `finish_child` at their wait, as the host's hook does, and the fork_join journey pins starts `a, b, c`. Not built: the `plan-child-submit` and `plan-fanout` confirmations the plan names, which are paid and user-run. growth +4060: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +565 (the stack of twenty-three stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D225, stands at +16273 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: the brief's submit lines, `Run._complete`'s child branch and `Todo.cancel`'s interrupt; weighed and kept: `stale_reminder`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.287.0 | 2026-09-22 | The engine starts ready delegated todos: no owner op is needed to hand an admitted todo to its child (D224, amends D212; Closes #484). This is stage G1 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`. `PlanEngine::apply` is now `apply_once` followed by one pass of the new `plan/schedule.rs`: it reads the family's journal, takes each delegated Pending todo `admitted` lets through, skips one with a pending `spawn_intent` (repair's road, so the `NeedsReconciliation` refusal is never re-recorded) and one whose start was refused and journaled for this attempt in this process, and applies `start` as the new `Actor::Engine`, journaled `engine`; `check_actor` admits it to `start`, `submit`, `done` and `fail`. The children it spawned join the op's `spawned`, a refused start is one `notices` line that the plan tool now prints, and the outcome's plan, ready and held lists are re-derived, so `dispatched` names only what the owner still steps. A child's op, a `view` and a delegate that hosts nothing (`Delegate::hosts`, false for the CLI's `Unhosted`, which would otherwise journal a failed spawn per call) schedule nothing; the probe tick calls `dispatch_ready_all` for a resumed session that issues no op. `block` is the hold and `unblock` the release, with no opt-out field and no lever. Deleted: `Delegate::follow_up` and its "admissible now ...; you start each one" steer, `illegal_hint`'s delegation clause, the plan tool description's "step single todos, hand one to a child" and "pending, running, done in order" sentences (now "Declare todos with contracts and delegations; the engine starts, verifies and accepts delegated ones. done closes your own todos."), and doctrine's pending-running-done sentence for the plan tool; the request budget falls 47954 to 47754 bytes. In the library `Run.launch` adopts an engine-started delegated todo instead of sending `start` (an owner `start` on a running todo is `illegal_step`), `in_order` and `_schedule` settle what the engine started, `review_pod` holds its declared arbiter with `block` so the engine does not run the template, and `shapes._abandon` drops a failed reader behind an edge on a todo still open, since a `retry` now hands it straight back to the engine; the fake host runs the same pass. What a caller sees: `init`, `append`, `retry`, `unblock` and the `done` that frees a slot now return the spawned children, and a script that sent `start` for a delegated todo gets `illegal_step`. Tests: `plan_ops::an_engine_start_is_journaled_as_engine` and `a_blocked_todo_is_not_started` (T0, new); rewritten to pin the engine-start rule, never weakened: `backpressure_holds_a_delegated_todo_pending` (init spawns the first, the owner's start is `illegal_step`, the freed slot starts the held one inside the `done`), `start_refuses_unmet_after_edges` (the waiting todo is delegated and starts when the edge clears), `a_pending_spawn_intent_refuses_a_second_start` (the engine skips the intent and records nothing; the owner's start is the one refusal), `a_ninth_delegated_start_is_refused_by_the_engine_with_the_count` (init spawns eight, no engine refusal), `mutation_is_owner_gated_and_unblock_is_open_to_user_and_host` (the Engine arm), `fuse_refuses_at_cap_and_survives_supersede`, `plan_recovery::ambiguous_spawn_is_reconciled_not_reissued`, `plan_program::program_records_source_before_the_first_effect`, `plan::dispatch::a_ready_todo_dispatches_a_child_and_reap_promotes_its_product`, the `campaign-decompose-and-supersede.json` start steps (now `illegal_step`), the fuzzer's touched model, and `test_yi_shapes`' fork_join order test; `the_follow_up_wakes_an_idle_owner_and_names_the_held_count` is deleted with the steer. Not built: the `plan-width-lever` and `plan-fanout` confirmations the plan names, which are paid and user-run. growth +3495: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +75 (the stack of twenty-two stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D224, stands at +15708 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: `Delegate::follow_up` and its implementations. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.286.0 | 2026-09-22 | A worktree delegation without a contract cannot be declared: the rule moves from a validation after parse into the type (D223, amends D194's declaration road; Closes #482). This is stage G0 of `docs/plans/2026-09-22-the-engine-runs-the-plan.md`, which lands with it. `crates/types/src/plan/op.rs::TodoSpec` is `#[serde(try_from = "TodoSpecRepr")]`, and the `TryFrom` refuses a `delegation` whose `isolation` is `worktree` beside no `contract`, naming the todo and the 0.285.0 text, now the one constant `UNCONTRACTED_WORKTREE`; `plan/tool.rs::todo_specs` builds the same repr, so `init`, `append`, `decompose` and `supersede` are refused as `bad_args` on the JSON tool and on `plan.op` alike, and a `set` row or a journal record carrying the shape fails to parse. `Todo`'s own `TryFrom<TodoRepr>` stays permissive, so a stored document and an `import` are read as they were written; `table.rs::validate_plan` holds every plan to the shape alone and `validate_shape` is gone; `table.rs::check_contracted` is the one backstop left, for `retry`, the op that swaps a delegation onto a todo without carrying its contract. In the library `Writer(accept: Contract, *, ...)` takes its contract as a required argument, `Role.accept` is the todo's contract unless `plan.todo(accept=)` names another, and `Plan._spec` raises `TypeError("a worktree Writer needs accept=")` before any request is sent; `help(yi)`'s `Writer`, `plan.todo` and `todo.retry` examples and `orchestrate.md`'s fork_join example carry `accept=` on the role, the request budget moves in its own ratchet, and every `Writer()` in the tests, the walkthrough program and the kernel journeys carries its contract. Tests: `plan_doc::an_uncontracted_worktree_spec_is_refused_at_parse_and_a_stored_todo_is_not` (T0, yi-types: the spec refuses, the tagged `append` refuses, the contracted spec and the stored todo parse), `plan/tool.rs::an_uncontracted_worktree_todo_is_refused_by_the_parse_on_every_declaring_op` (T0: four ops through `parse_op` and the request road's `bad_args`, and an inline delegation still needs no contract), `plan_ops::contracts::a_retry_swapping_in_an_uncontracted_worktree_delegation_is_refused` (T0, the rewrite of 0.285.0's `a_worktree_delegation_with_no_contract_is_refused_where_it_is_declared`, whose `init` and `append` arms the type now makes unreachable), and `test_yi_plan::test_a_writer_without_accept_is_refused_before_the_host` (T0: `Writer()` is a `TypeError`, `Writer(accept=None)` on a worktree todo is refused with nothing sent, an inline writer still declares without one, and the role's contract is the todo's). Behaviour an existing caller can see: a journal written before 0.285.0 with an uncontracted worktree todo, which 0.285.0 already refused at reduce as a `Contract` op error, now fails the record's parse instead. Not built: the `plan-child-submit` confirmation the plan names, which is paid and user-run. growth +3420: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +86 (the stack of twenty-one stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D223, stands at +15633 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`); weighed for deletion and taken: `validate_shape` and the whole-plan scan in `validate_plan`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.285.0 | 2026-09-21 | A worktree delegation with no contract is refused where it is declared (Closes #479). `plan/table.rs::validate_plan` refuses it on `init`, `append`, `set` and the `retry` that swaps a worktree delegation onto an uncontracted todo, naming the missing `contract` and that a delegation `accept` is the child's brief and not one; `import` is held to the shape alone through the new `validate_shape`, so a legacy document is still read as it was written. `PlanOpError::AcceptanceUnavailable` names the road for the todos a store already holds: a `set` row carries the contract in, `submit` follows, and `fail` or `drop` takes the disposition road. Both dogfood owners in the #478 pass declared exactly the refused shape, so no child of theirs could have submitted or completed whatever it did. The campaign walkthrough fixture declared it too and now delegates without a worktree. Tests: `plan_ops::contracts::a_worktree_delegation_with_no_contract_is_refused_where_it_is_declared`, with `set_cannot_complete_a_worktree_todo` moved onto a contracted todo. Not built: a contract in the walkthrough fixture harness, which parses no contract at all. growth +3334: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +23 (the stack of twenty stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D222, stands at +15547 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`). |
| 0.284.0 | 2026-09-21 | A dispatched child can take both submit roads (D222, narrows D211; Closes #478). `plan/request.rs::own_product` admits a non-owner's `artifacts` rider only for `Op::Submit`, one blob, on a todo the stored plan says is `Running { by }` that child at the named attempt; another todo's attempt, a two-blob batch, a `done` and every other actor still read "only the plan owner stores artifacts", now under the `not_owner` code because the rider refuses the principal rather than the arguments. `yi/plan.py::_record` swallows that one code and no other: a child's cells belong to its own session, not to the plan's program, and the record was dying before `Todo.submit` reached its own op. `affordance::call_template` keeps every argument the caller sent, including the ones the flat plan schema does not name: the 0.283.0 dogfood's repeated-refusal template printed a submit with `attempt` dropped and the parser then refused the call the surface had just shown. The worktree brief names the kernel-cell road again, which mints the product url two children got wrong by hand. Tests: `plan_e2e::a_child_stores_the_product_of_the_attempt_it_submits`, `plan::tool::tests::a_submit_template_carries_the_attempt_its_parser_needs` with the template round trip inside `every_fixture_op_argument_set_parses`, `test_yi_plan::test_a_child_records_no_cell_and_still_submits_its_product`. Not built: matching the submitted url against the blob's own digest (the op refuses an output it cannot resolve, and the running check is what bounds the write). No prompt byte moved: the request budget stays 47954, system 28124 and tools 19830. growth +3311: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline, of which this row is +89 (the stack of twenty stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D222, stands at +15524 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`). No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.283.0 | 2026-09-21 | Nine tool-ergonomics and plan-engine fixes from a 105-session corpus: a refusal now names the caller's own mistake, `bash` runs bash, and a delegated todo can be accepted (D221, part of D219; Closes #468, #469, #470, #471, #472, #473, #474, #475, #476, #477). `write` returns the `[path#TAG]` header it already minted, so the tag an edit must cite exists (30 false edit refusals in the corpus invented one); the header refusal and the "not from this session" text name `write`; an edit rebases the prior snapshot's seen lines through `rebase::LineMap` so an unchanged line the model saw stays an anchor (10 more); the overlap refusal names the single `PUT` a `CUT`+`PUT` pair meant. `todo::locate` normalises both sides (a trailing ellipsis, backticks, whitespace) before comparing, so a label the renderer cut names its item back (11), and `unkey_op` reads `{"done": "t3"}` as `op` plus `id` for the six ops whose one argument is the item (4). A child's fenced JSON answer is read as JSON (one fence line and any trailer stripped, first value taken; prose still refuses with its text) (10). `jobs.rs::interpreter` probes `command -v bash` once and spawns it, with a POSIX-sh annotation where bash is absent (11); the `[chain stopped]` line states the rule instead of claiming a fact that was false in 15 of 59 cases, and the duplicated chain-stop paragraph left `prompts/doctrine.md`, so the request budget falls 48182 to 47954 bytes. `dispatch_width` is `min(family.max_children, plan.width_max)` and takes no argument; the pinned `plan_ops::dispatch_width` test is rewritten as `width_is_the_family_cap_not_the_host` with the reason in its own comment. A worktree delegation's brief names the submit verb and what it carries, and only a worktree one; the dispatch notice says who must act. Refusal texts that now name the road: plan `done` on a pending todo, `output` as prose, a `set` label measured against its cap, a `set` with no open plan, a todo spec's shape, `evidence` on `done`, an inline note over 1024 (the cap stays: forty noted delegations have a 32 KiB frontmatter budget, and big content travels by url), `Plan.create` on an open plan, a held worktree, and a present wrong-typed argument reported as missing. One graph edge carries the 600 s cell ceiling on `result_error(aborted)`, which `cell_output` now sets; module-level `rlm.result` takes `timeout`. Tests: `tools::a_write_result_carries_the_tag_an_edit_must_cite`, `a_placeholder_tag_refusal_names_where_the_tag_comes_from`, `an_edit_carries_the_seen_lines_it_did_not_move`, `bash_runs_process_substitution`, `a_wrong_typed_argument_is_not_reported_as_missing`, `todo_e2e::a_label_the_renderer_cut_names_its_item_back`, `an_op_passed_as_a_key_lands_as_that_op`, `plan::dispatch::a_fenced_json_answer_validates_against_a_schema`, `a_worktree_brief_names_the_submit_verb`, `plan_ops::refusals` (five), `plan_ops::width_is_the_family_cap_not_the_host`. Every refusal stays a refusal: an ambiguous todo prefix, prose where JSON was required, a `set` carrying no id, and a rewritten line the model never saw are all still refused. The reading that produced those nine issues is now a standing loop rather than a scratchpad driver: `evals/surface.py` runs one rollout per scenario, each in its own workspace and HOME as `run.py` does, and scores the sessions by the refusals they recorded. Scenarios are data (`evals/fixtures/surface/scenarios.json`: road, prompt, seed files, optional `levers` for an `--eval` run, and what a clean run looks like), so a new road is a JSON entry and never an edit to the runner. Each point also reports the median paired difference per task beside the pooled interval, and a grid whose repetitions and tasks cannot make six pairs says so on stderr before it spends anything. A trial row whose metric is a string, a boolean, a NaN or an infinity is refused rather than compared (a NaN reward passes a floor by not being a number), and a candidate that did not run a task the baseline ran is `task_not_run:<task>`, never a saving. An unknown lever, one marked not tunable (its `why` is printed), a non-integer or an out-of-range value is refused before the runner is called, by the rules `Levers::load` applies to the same file. The runner is the owner's command, `<runner> <overrides.json> <task>...` through an argv list, with `YI_LEVERS` naming the same file in the child's environment and this process's own untouched; `--group validation` is selection and the result says which group ran. No least-squares fit over the manifest and no Gaussian process; a later optimizer is its own proposal. Tests on a synthetic runner: `a_comparison_reports_its_interval` (nine pairs, the interleaved order, a two-pair saving that stays inconclusive, a noisy one, a failing one), `a_grid_refuses_a_knob_marked_not_tunable` (and the run bound, the knob bound, spend counted), `a_candidate_outside_its_range_is_refused`. Limits: the pooled interval treats pairs from one task as independent of each other, which is why the per-task medians ride beside it, and it is over the runs that were made, not over unseen tasks; the interval is for the median, so a saving on a minority of tasks reads inconclusive; the final group is not reachable from the command line. Proposals are data: a JSONL of `add_edge`, `drop_edge` and `reword` with the full edge and a rationale, at most 20 a run, an edge addressed by `(from, relation, to, condition)`. `read_proposals` refuses the whole file when a line names a validation or final task of `evals/levers/split.json`: the line is decoded and matched by token, so an id written as a JSON escape is still the id and an id inside a longer word is not a reference; a development task may be named. Per edit, in order and fail closed: the rejection memory (the same `editHash` under the same `baseVersion`, `protocol` and `model` is skipped before anything runs; a rejection under another configuration counts in `earlierRejections` and the edit runs), `apply` (`malformed_edit`, `edge_exists`, `no_such_edge`, `no_change`), then `check`, which is `Graph::check` rule for rule plus `parse` for what serde refuses, so an invalid edit is rejected under the rule's name and never reaches a run. That is the efficiency gate: a line that grows the rendered bytes rides every request that carries it, so it is promoted only when the tasks solved pay for it, and each verdict records the edit's `guidanceBytes` delta. A promoted edit is the next base at `version + 1`. Rejections are appended to `evals/fixtures/graph/rejected.jsonl` as `{editHash, baseVersion, protocol, model, reason, scores, at}`; the hash covers the kind and the edge, not the rationale. `--write` writes the graph in `dump`'s form, one node and one edge per line, and `graph.json` was rewritten into it (the same JSON value; F4a's byte-identical test passes). The two rule sets cannot drift quietly: `evals/fixtures/graph/structural.json` holds the closed condition list and 21 graphs, valid at every limit and invalid one rule at a time, and `graph::the_shared_fixture_is_judged_alike_on_both_sides` (yi-types) and the Python test of the same name both judge it; moving `MAX_GUIDANCE_BYTES` on one side was watched red. `split.json`, shared with F4c, draws development and validation over the seven synthetic tasks of `evals/fixtures/tasks`, final empty (section 10.4). Beside it, every fact a seam asserts is now spelled once in `yi_types::graph` (`RESULT_OK`, `RESULT_ERROR`, `GRID_ANSWER_EMPTY`, `COROUTINE_UNAWAITED`, `METHOD_AWAITED`, `LISTING_NAME_MISSED`, `SESSION_ON_DISK`, `SESSION_IN_MEMORY`, `CHILD_RUNNING`, `CHILD_FINISHED`, `CHILD_STATE`, `TODO_STATE`), and `PREDICATES` is built from the same constants, so a mistyped fact cannot compile into a line that silently stops firing; `graph::every_fact_a_seam_asserts_parses` covers the two the seams compose an argument onto. | 0.279.0 | 2026-09-21 | The host's next-step lines are a procedural graph the binary carries, and the string builders that wrote them are gone (D219; Closes #463). This is stage F4a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 9.1 to 9.4, 9.7, 11 and 12). `crates/types/src/graph.rs` holds `Graph { version, nodes, edges }`: a node names one of Yi's verbs (`tool`, `op` or `request`), an edge is `(from, relation, to)` with a `condition`, `guidance`, `pitfalls` and a `weight`, and the relations are `then`, `instead`, `before`, `after_error` and `after_refusal`. A condition is a `Predicate`, a string that parses only when it is a name from the closed table `PREDICATES` with one of the arguments that name takes (`always`, `result_ok`, `result_error(<kind>)`, `output_capped`, `todo_open`, `todo_state(running|pending|blocked)`, `plan_ready_nonempty`, `child_state(<state>)`, `blocked_on(user|child|external)`, `worktree_unmerged`, `done_refused`, `inbox_nonempty`, and the six states the old producers branched on: `coroutine_unawaited`, `method_awaited`, `listing_name_missed`, `grid_answer_empty`, `session_on_disk`, `session_in_memory`); there is no expression language, unknown fields are refused, and no model evaluates a condition: the host asserts the predicates that hold as facts at the seam and the renderer compares names. `Graph::check(verbs)` holds the structural rules of section 9.4 in a fixed order and returns the name of the one that failed (`unregistered_node`, `too_many_edges`, `unknown_node`, `self_edge`, `too_many_out_edges`, `guidance_size`, `pitfalls_size`, `unreachable`), which is what the offline refiner of F4b records. `crates/runtime/src/prompts/graph.json` is compiled in with `include_str!` and parsed once behind a `OnceLock` (`affordance::shipped`): no file read at run time, no environment variable, no reload; a graph that failed to parse would render nothing, and the structural test is what keeps that from shipping. `affordance::render(graph, last_call, facts)` localizes by exact match on the last call, walks two hops of edges whose conditions hold, sorts by weight, substitutes the one `{name}` a line cites, deduplicates by text and truncates at `facts.cap`: `TOOL_LINES` (2) for a tool result, and the todo tool keeps `NEXT_LINES` (3) in `next_lines`, which renders one item at a time. `affordance::next(last_call, holds, name)` is the joined form the spawn reply, the child notice and the compaction notice use. The migration is byte-identical: `affordance::every_line_rendered_today_renders_from_the_graph` pinned every line of `spawned`, `coroutine_leak`, `method_awaited`, `listing_name`, `child_finished`, `grid_empty`, both branches of `compacted`, `call_template` and the todo tool's `moves` (ids and quoted labels, the cap of three, the blocked fallback, a name that contains `{name}`) as golden bytes against the old producers in its own commit, and the same goldens pass from the graph. With that the seven string builders in `affordance.rs` and `todo/text.rs::moves`'s match were dead and are deleted; `NEXT`, `append` and `call_template` (derived from a schema, never a fixed string) stay, and `moves` is now four lines over the renderer. `tools.rs` replaces `grid_note` and `ipython_note` with `facts_of`, which asserts `result_ok` or `result_error(<errorKind>)`, `grid_answer_empty` and at most one ipython needle, in the order the old code tried them, so one result still carries one line. The spawn reply localizes at `rlm.run` for `rlm.service` too, because `service::handle` is one function behind one admission. Tests (T0): `affordance::the_shipped_graph_passes_every_structural_check` checks the shipped graph against the builtin tool names, `ipython`, `plan`, `todo`, the todo ops and every host request read from the runtime's own `register("…")` calls; `affordance::an_unknown_last_call_renders_nothing`; `affordance::rendering_stops_at_two_hops_and_three_lines`; `graph::an_unknown_predicate_fails_to_parse` parses every name and argument in the table and refuses eleven near misses; every earlier affordance string test is kept, pointed at the renderer. Behaviour an existing caller can see: none; `tool_surface.json`, `request_budget.json` and the behaviour cassettes are unchanged, and `schemas.lock` gains the six `graph::` shapes. The kernel-dead path: the graph is host data and renders for the JSON tools the same. Limits: the facts `output_capped`, `todo_open`, `plan_ready_nonempty`, `blocked_on`, `worktree_unmerged`, `done_refused` and `inbox_nonempty` are vocabulary no seam asserts yet, so an edge on one never fires until a later stage asserts it; the todo producer localizes at the tool, not the op, because its lines are the same after every op; plan ops are not nodes until an edge needs one. Not built: the value comparison of the strings, a plain rule table and the graph on the same tasks (section 9.1), which is a paid measurement and the owner's; until its row exists the graph has earned only equivalence. growth +2727: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this stage is +207 against 270 estimated (the stack of fifteen stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D219, stands at +14940 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): 180 lines in yi-types against 90, of which the predicate table is 22 and `check` 46, and 27 net in yi-runtime against 180 (121 added, 94 deleted), because the deleted builders paid for the renderer; the procedural graph and its renderer, and the affordance strings became data. | 0.278.0 | 2026-09-21 | A service is a child with a stable address: its name is reserved, a crashed run is respawned under it with the transcript and inbox kept, within a restart intensity and on a freshly drawn lease (D218, extends D165, D214, D215 and D216; Closes #462). This is stage F3c of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 4.2, 7.1, 7.3 and 11). `rlm.service(name, brief, restart=3, **kwargs)` sends the new host request `rlm.service`; `SubagentHost::service` (`crates/runtime/src/subagent/service.rs`) refuses an empty name, `parent`, `all` and `isolation="worktree"` (a respawn has no lane to settle the last incarnation's checkout into), attaches when a live service holds the name with the same brief (`attached: true`, the same child id), refuses another brief, and otherwise admits through `admit`, the body `spawn` and `spawn_seated` already shared. An ordinary `rlm.run` asking for a service's name meets the spawn's own `already taken` refusal, which holds through a respawn because the record never leaves the roster. The respawn is `SubagentHost::respawn`, called from `conclude` (the tail of `run_child`, split out so a woken turn can reach it) and deciding under the roster lock: only `Failed { Provider }` and `Failed { KernelDeath }` respawn, never `Completed`, `Interrupted`, `Reaped`, `Repossessed`, a red check, a refused spawn or a deadline; never a service `revoke` or the parent's `close` marked stopped; and never past `restart` respawns inside `RESTART_WINDOW_MS` (600,000), which `MAX_RESTARTS` caps at ten so a payload cannot ask for a crash loop (refused, never clamped). It returns the dead incarnation's lease (`return_lease`, journaled as F2b journals every return), draws the next with the kept kwargs through `draw`, so an ask the parent can no longer cover is refused with both numbers and never clamped, builds the session outside the lock (`build.rs`: `cast` and `build`, moved out of `spawn_seated`), attaches it to the same session store, reads the stopped mark again under the lock before installing it (the build is the one stretch a respawn holds no lock across, so a `revoke` or a `close` that lands there ends the service rather than being overruled, and the built session's kernel is disposed, as the dead incarnation's is), and steps the record with `Step::Respawn`: exit cleared, phase `Queued`, the counters reset and `billed_from` set to the kept transcript's length, which `refold` and the lease return both count from, so a lagged watch cannot charge a predecessor's turns to the successor's lease (`recursion_e2e::a_respawned_service_is_billed_from_its_own_first_turn`). No terminal update is published for a run that was respawned, so F1e's one ending per record holds. A refused respawn leaves the record `Failed` with `<cause>; not respawned: <why>` as its error and its notice (`recursion_e2e::a_service_out_of_restarts_or_lease_ends_failed_and_says_so`). A service's later turns have no `run_child` behind them, so `watch` hands `AgentEnd` to `turn_ended`, which reads the settled turn's exit for a service whose first run completed and sends a crash down the same road, one reader at a time (mail can start a turn on a run that already crashed, and two readers of that one crash would end it twice) (`recursion_e2e::a_woken_crash_respawns_and_a_parent_close_ends_a_service_for_good`). `ChildRecord.juror` is now `standing: Standing { Worker, Juror, Service(Service) }`. `reserve` counts only workers against `max_children` and caps only workers, so a service never reaped does not pin a slot and eight retained workers do not refuse one; the depth exemption stays a juror's alone, a service takes no `Purpose::Verification` seat (it has no seat argument), and the family cap, the lease and the wall bind it like any child (`recursion_e2e::a_service_is_outside_the_worker_cap_and_under_the_depth_limit`). An idle service's `Completed` exit sends no lifecycle notice, since there is nothing to reap; a question it ended on still reaches the parent. `yi_types::mail::Envelope` gains `fromIncarnation` and `toIncarnation`, both `Option<u32>` and omitted when absent, so an inbox or a fixture written before this stage parses and re-serializes byte for byte (`mail::an_envelope_round_trips_in_the_wire_spelling_the_inbox_stores`); `schemas.lock` moved for `mail::Envelope` alone. The host fills both from the registry at `Desk::seal`: a service's incarnation, `None` for the parent, an ordinary child and an address nobody live holds. `present` shows `from_incarnation="n"` and `to_incarnation="n"` on a message a service sent or was addressed, so the incarnation that reads a kept transcript can tell its predecessor's conversation from its own. A kept inbox therefore says which incarnation each message was addressed to, the respawn's brief tells the new incarnation that mail addressed to an earlier one was its predecessor's conversation, the dead session's queues die with it so nothing is presented to the successor on its own, and a request parked on the dead incarnation is refused at once by name (`"index" was respawned before it replied to <id>`). `status()` members carry `service` and `incarnation`. Test: `recursion_e2e::a_service_respawns_under_its_name_and_keeps_its_inbox` (T1) holds incarnation 1 open on a tool call, sends it mail and a request, asks for the name through `rlm.run`, attaches through the registered `rlm.service` request, lets the run crash, and reads the refusal of the parked request, one record, both incarnations' stamps in one inbox, `history://index` with both briefs, and no notice; every control above was watched red with its line removed. Behaviour an existing caller can see: `rlm.status()` members gain two keys; a worker spawn's validation order is unchanged; `tool_surface.json` is unchanged (the kernel surface is not a tool); no prompt fragment moved. The kernel-dead path: a service is a kernel call, and with no kernel there is no `rlm` at all; mail to a name and `history://<name>` keep working from the JSON tools. Limits: restart counts and incarnations live in the host and restart with it, as its children do; the respawned session loads its predecessor's transcript, so a crash caused by that context recurs until compaction or the intensity ends it; a turn that reported no usage (every provider error) leaves that incarnation's returned lease wholly spent, F2b's rule; a service cannot hold a worktree; and the waiter carries no incarnation of its own, because the respawn retires every waiter on the name first. Not built: a health `cmd` and a shutdown contract for a service (section 6.2's table row), and adoption of a service by a restarted host. growth +2520: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this stage is +430 (the stack of fourteen stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D218, stands at +14733 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): 424 lines in yi-runtime against 140 estimated and 6 in yi-types, with 24 of Python against 40. | 0.277.0 | 2026-09-21 | A review pod is a recipe: readers with distinct briefs whose findings are evidence, and one arbiter whose command is the verdict (D217, extends D212 and D216; Closes #461). This is stage F3b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 8.4, 8.5 and 11). `python/yi_runtime/src/yi/recipes/review_pod.py` is the first file under `yi/recipes/`. `declare(plan, subject, check, arbiter=None)` declares one `Reader` per brief in `BRIEFS` (correctness, tests, scope), each bound to `subject` with its brief as the role's note and answering `shapes.ANSWER`, and an `arbiter` todo after them, a worktree `Writer` unless another role is named, whose contract is `check`. `review_pod(plan, run)` is module-level like the shapes, so the run lease's identity check attaches a second `plan.run(shape=review_pod)` to the first. It is no third scheduler: the readers run under `shapes._schedule` with its one_for_one restart, each answer goes through `shapes._survivor` (a failed reader is retried and dropped so the plan can finish, a null answer abstains, and a finding survives only with a quote `verify_quotes` found on the cited line inside that reader's own partition), and the arbiter is issued by `shapes._asked`, which gained a `context` argument. No plan op rewrites a declared todo's note, so the arbiter runs as `<key>-r2`, the way scatter issues a reader again per round: the issue copies the declared delegation and contract, carries the surviving findings in `delegation.note` cut to the host's 1 KiB `InlineNote` cap (on a whole byte sequence, and the cut says it happened, because half a finding reads as a different finding), and carries every reader's whole answer as `context` urls; the declared arbiter is then dropped. The issue is started once under `_schedule` with no restart, so it is verified once: a red command fails the pod and nothing asks again. Geometry is checked before the first start and names every problem at once: fewer than two readers, a reader with no note or with another reader's note, a reader with no `schema` item, anything but exactly one todo that is not a reader, an arbiter with no delegate, an arbiter with no critical `cmd` or `example` item, and an arbiter carrying a `judge` item (a judged arbiter meets no floor, and one verdict would spend the todo's juries). Tests: `test_yi_shapes::test_a_pod_verdict_is_the_arbiters_command_not_a_reader` (T0: two blockers and a green command pass, three clean readers and a red command fail, an invented quote never reaches the note, one start and no retry), `test_yi_shapes::test_a_pod_without_a_code_arbiter_or_distinct_briefs_is_refused` (T0), and the journey `kernel_data_surface::review_pod_on_the_fixture_repo` (T2: the recipe in a real kernel over `fixtures/plans/worktree/repo.sh`, the real step table and the real `cmd` verifier; the journey's rig moved into a `crewed` helper the F1b journey now shares). Behaviour an existing caller can see: `yi.review_pod` is a new public name with a docstring example, and `help(yi)` lists it; nothing else moved, no prompt fragment changed and `tool_surface.json` is unchanged. The kernel-dead path is the JSON plan tool declaring the same todos by hand; a pod is policy in user space and the engine knows nothing of it. Not built: one_for_all restart on the passes, which section 8.5 names and F1b did not ship; a calibrated findings contract (a finding is the `ANSWER` shape scatter already had, one answer and its quotes); and a record of which model a reader executed on, which F3a's limits hand to this stage: the recipe sees the role's declared `model`, which is usually absent, and `rlm.result` returns no model, so the jury's owner family is still the host's own and the delegation's selector. growth +2090: measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this stage is +0 (the stack of thirteen stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D217, stands at +14303 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): no Rust `src` line moved; Python is 128 lines against 200 estimated and is not counted. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.276.0 | 2026-09-20 | A judged contract item is a walled reader of another model family answering a fixed schema, its quotes checked against its fetch log, aggregated in Rust (D216, extends D194 and D215; Closes #460). This is stage F3a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 6.4 and 11). The `judge` decider has been on the wire since F0c and refused at declaration; `Contract::validate` now accepts it with a jury of one or three (`ContractError::JurySize` for any other size, replacing `JudgeUnavailable`) and still refuses one that stands alone, because a judge item is neither behavioural nor shape and meets no floor (`contract::validate_enforces_the_floors_and_a_judge_never_stands_alone`). The jury is `crates/runtime/src/plan/judge.rs`, one more decider bound into `Verifier::run` through the `Judge` trait F0c left open; there is no second verification path. `Jury::judge` seats `policy.n` children through `SubagentHost::spawn_seated`, each on a model of another family than the owner's. The family is the vendor segment of a registry model (`subagent/models.rs`: `family_of`, `other_families`, with `find_models` moved beside them): `openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` is `z-ai` and `openrouter/anthropic/...` is `anthropic`. The owners are the host's own model, read from its settings, and the selector the plan spawned the todo's child with, resolved in the registry; nothing a payload says names a family, and with no other family on offer the item abstains `no other family` and nothing is spawned (`judge::a_judge_is_another_family_or_the_item_abstains`). The candidates are the registry's models on the host's own provider or on a provider whose key is set, cheapest input first. The brief (`prompts/judge.md`, a user message to the juror and no part of any cached prefix) is the rubric, the evidence addresses and the schema; `brief` takes nothing else, the spawn carries no `fork` and no `context`, and the evidence is served through the juror's own `fetch`, never inlined (`judge::the_brief_carries_no_history_or_prior_verdict`). The juror's wall denies every write under the workspace and every URL scheme but `local://`; it is cooperative, as every wall is. The data rule is `auto_review.md`'s, word for word. The answer is `yi_types::plan::contract::JurorAnswer`, parsed with `deny_unknown_fields`: an empty answer, a bare word, fenced JSON, trailing prose, a missing or unknown key and a fourth verdict word each abstain, and so does a decided vote that quotes nothing (`judge::malformed_or_empty_answers_abstain`). Every quote must name an evidence address, the juror's own transcript must hold a whole read of it at the frozen hash (`fetch::rows_of`, `fetch::as_served`), and the quoted line must be that line, which a blank one never is; any miss abstains the whole item, above the tally, and marks the dropped juror's line with the check's own `unbacked` flag rather than a prefix of the reason the juror wrote (`judge::an_unbacked_quote_abstains_the_item`, the `quotes` rows of `fixtures/plans/judge/jurors.json`). Evidence that tells its juror to pass and quote nothing therefore produces an abstention from a juror that obeys and a backed fail from one that does not (`judge::evidence_carrying_instructions_is_data`, the `steered` rows). `tally` computes the quorum over the seats asked for: one judge decides alone, of three two like votes decide, and one pass with two abstentions abstains (`judge::one_pass_and_two_abstentions_abstain`). Each juror's model, vote, clipped reason and `unbacked` flag ride `ItemLine.jurors` into the journal's verdict, and the `done` or `done_refused` record a session sees carries the counts as `jurors`, which `extract.py` reads as `juror_pass`, `juror_fail`, `juror_abstain` and `quotes_dropped`. Bounds: the done path reserves `Purpose::Verification` for a judged contract and hands the permit to the jury as its `Seat`; `spawn_seated` reads the permit's purpose, and a juror neither fills the worker cap nor is refused by it or by the depth limit, while the family cap and the owner's lease (100,000 tokens a juror, returned at retire) still bind (`judge::full_capacity_still_adjudicates_through_the_reservation`). A juror is retired, never reaped, and its ending is not a notice to the owner it judged. `juries` counts the `verification_requested` records on the todo at the plan's version; past `JUDGE_CAP_PER_TODO` (3) `Verifier::run` escalates the item without seating anyone, and `refuse` blocks a todo on the user at once on an `Escalate` outcome, under a note that names the escalation instead of a refusal tally that never ran (`plan_ops::the_fourth_jury_on_one_todo_escalates_to_the_user`). The whole-verification deadline is read once ahead of every decider rather than inside `run_item`, so no jury is seated for an item with no time left to read it. Handed down by F2b and built: the engine's spawn road computes the child's effective wall (`SubagentHost::wall_for`, which `spawn` now shares) and refuses a delegation whose `context` names a URL that wall denies, the parent's own wall included (`judge::a_brief_naming_context_its_wall_denies_is_refused`). Behaviour an existing caller can see: a contract with a `judge` item is declarable; an `Escalate` verdict blocks its todo on the user on the first refusal rather than the third; a delegation whose context its wall denies is refused the spawn where it used to fail one fetch later; `done` and `done_refused` session records may carry `jurors`. No prompt fragment of the working model moved and `tool_surface.json` is unchanged. Limits a deployment reaches before calibration does: a `plans.dir` outside the cwd puts the evidence blobs outside the tree a juror reads, so every judged item abstains there; a host whose own grant is smaller than three juror leases has a seat refused and abstains by quorum; and a todo the plan holds no delegation for contributes no owner family beyond the host's own model, because `spawn_result` records the agent name and not the model a child executed on. Not built, and said in the plan: a jury on the worktree paths (`submit`'s candidate check and the staging check pass no seat, so a judged item abstains there; seating one on each would spend two of a todo's three juries on one submit, which needs a decision first); a `judge(...)` builder in the `yi` library (a judged item is declared through the contract's JSON; section 6.4 puts calibration before default use); a `judge` record kind of its own in `ops.jsonl` (the juror lines ride the verdict the `done` and `done_refused` records already carry) and a `todo.juries` field (the count is derived from the journal); a fragment or paged read as backing for a quote (the hash is the whole file's, so a juror that reads a span is unbacked and abstains); a configured judge model (cheapest other family is the reader rule); a `kernel dead` abstention of its own (a juror that cannot fetch cannot quote, and abstains by the quote rule); and the plan journal's `Disposition::RepossessionPending`, which still needs a road from `SubagentHost` into the engine's journal: this stage opened the opposite road, from the engine's verifier into the host, and the repossession happens on the probe loop's timer where no engine transaction is open. growth +2090: three stages measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this one is +596 (the stack of twelve stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D216, stands at +14303 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): 549 lines in yi-runtime against 320 estimated and 47 in yi-types, with 7 of Python. The overshoot is the seat (the verification permit carried from the done path through `Snapshot` into `spawn_seated`, which the estimate took for granted), the jury count and the escalation arm in `done.rs`, the juror lines and their session-visible counts, and the context refusal handed down by F2b; weighed for deletion and taken: the tests live in `tests/judge.rs` and not in `src`, `find_models` moved out of `subagent.rs` so that file shrank by 6 lines, `relevance_of` now reads through `rows_of` and does not share a copy of the loop, the juror schema is a serde type in yi-types and no hand parser, the jury cap sits in the verifier so the engine test needs no host, and `spawn` and the delegate share one `wall_for`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.275.0 | 2026-09-20 | A child holds a lease drawn from its parent; revoke has a grace and a visible repossession record; walls shrink hereditarily and holds compile down at spawn; and a child ends on one typed exit every surface reads the same way (D215, extends D165, D210 and D214; Closes #459). This is stage F2b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 7.4 to 7.6 and 11) with the child lifecycle audit's items a to e. The exit: `yi_types::subagent::ChildExit` (`Completed`, `Failed { class }`, `Interrupted`, `Reaped`, `Repossessed`) replaces F1e's forced status; `family::read_exit` is the one function that turns it into the wire `ChildStatus`, the `MemberState` and the notice's verb (`subagent::status_state_and_notice_derive_from_one_exit`). `ChildStatus` keeps its three words and `ChildUpdate` gains an optional `exit`, so an older client reads what it read. `ChildRecord::step` in the new `crates/runtime/src/subagent/record.rs` is the one writer of a record's lifecycle fields; the watch, `run_child`, `deliver_to_parent`, the lane restore and `retire` call it. A member admitted and not yet polled reads `queued`, which `yi.plan`'s scheduler waits on (`test_yi_plan.test_a_queued_child_is_waited_on`). `stuck` is read from a typed `LoopSignal` the loop and the todo coupling write beside their records, never from a record's name (`family::stuck_reads_the_typed_signal`). The TUI's stop and ACP's `_yi/child_abort` go through `SubagentHost::interrupt`, and `ChildView.session` is a `ChildFeed` that can subscribe and read and cannot abort (`recursion_e2e::a_tui_stop_is_a_host_interrupt`). The lease: `crates/types/src/lease.rs` is the wire (`Lease`, `Revocation`, `Repossession`, `Returned`, `LeaseRecord`, `ParentClose`) and `crates/runtime/src/lease.rs` the mechanism. `rlm.run` takes `deadline_s`, `tokens` and `parent_close`, and the engine's dispatch draws `SpawnSpec.budget` as its child's `tokens`, so both spawn roads are leased (`plan::dispatch::kwargs_draw_the_specs_budget_as_the_childs_lease`); `SubagentHost::draw` runs under the roster lock, refuses an ask past the parent's clock less a 30 s cleanup grace or past its unreserved tokens with both numbers, never clamps, and gives an omitted deadline the inherited bound, which becomes the child session's own `deadline:` clock (`subagent::a_deadline_past_the_parents_bound_is_refused_with_both_numbers`, `subagent::a_spawn_asking_past_the_parents_deadline_or_tokens_is_refused_with_both_numbers`). The reservation returns once as the record leaves, journaled with what was spent; a turn that reported no usage returns nothing (`plan_ledger::reap_records_the_unspent_lease`). `rlm.revoke(name, grace_s, reason)` journals the revocation on the parent's transcript as `custom{lease}`, sends a `cancel`, and registers the grace with the probe loop's `wake_at`; a revoked child is admitted no new work. The ladder's lease job runs on every wake before the in-flight-probe check, so a slow probe never delays an expiry (`plan_probe::a_revoke_due_time_wakes_the_loop_past_a_slow_probe`). At expiry the run is stopped and joined, the lane settles as `Retained` so uncommitted work is committed on its branch (`lanes::a_repossessed_worktree_keeps_its_work_on_its_branch`), the `Repossession` is journaled inside `retire_as` before the record is released or the terminal update published, and the parent gets one `failure` (`recursion_e2e::revoke_delivers_cancel_then_repossesses_after_grace_with_the_record_first`). A stop, settle or journal write that fails leaves the member `repossession_pending` with its worktree and record intact, retried on the next wake (a journal write that fails after the lane has already settled is the one loss: the retry's record names the transcript and not the branch, which is kept and reaped as an orphan lane) (`recursion_e2e::a_failed_settle_reports_repossession_pending_with_references_kept`); the first expiry after a restart completes any revocation the journal left open (`recursion_e2e::restart_during_grace_completes_the_repossession`). `SpawnSpec.parent_close` is `terminate` with a grace (default 30 s) or `request_cancel`; `abandon` is refused until a supervisor exists (`subagent::abandon_is_refused_until_a_supervisor_exists`); `SubagentHost::close` applies it and ACP calls it when a session handle drops. `recursion_e2e::children_at_parent_close_today` was written first and pins that a dropped host handle still stops nothing, then the default `close`. A child's wall is its own under its parent's (`Wall::under`, `subagent::a_child_cannot_spawn_with_a_smaller_wall_than_its_parent`); `gate::compile_ask` denies a detached child's `Ask` with the hazard named, and the broker's no-asker arm now reads its refusal from it (`gate::a_detached_childs_ask_compiles_to_deny_and_an_attached_ones_stays_a_question`). From the F2a review: a removed or failed respondent refuses its waiters at once and names how it ended (`recursion_e2e::a_terminated_respondent_refuses_its_waiters_by_name`); a `cancel` starts no turn, sets a flag the loop reads at its message boundary, and the run reads `interrupted` with cause `cancelled` (`recursion_e2e::a_cancel_ends_the_run_at_its_next_message_boundary`); a child's own `failure` is a verdict, not an ending: it reads `failed` in `wait` at once while its run keeps its absent exit, and the run's own end turns it into `Failed { red_check }`, so one run still publishes one terminal update and a revoked child cannot declare itself out of its grace (`recursion_e2e::a_failure_from_a_child_reads_failed_in_wait`, and the dodge inside `revoke_delivers_cancel_then_repossesses_after_grace_with_the_record_first`); the cancel's receipt word comes from `follow_up_message`, never a status read. Behaviour an existing caller can see: `rlm.status` and `rlm.wait` may answer `queued` and `repossession_pending`; a delegation that names a `budget` is refused the spawn when its parent cannot lease it, where the number used to ride the brief as advice; a child deleted while running carries `exit: reaped` beside the unchanged `error: interrupted`; the environment's children line may open with a `queued` group; the prompt fragments are untouched. Not built, and said in the plan: the attached child's `Ask` as a `request` envelope (the family shares one broker, so the question already reaches the root's surface), the refusal of a spawn whose brief names a walled path, token exhaustion ending a run (tokens are reserved and accounted, not enforced mid-run), process-group kill beyond what `abort` already does to bash and the kernel cell, re-adopting an orphan lane after a restart (the record names it; `yi lanes` reaps it), `close` on the CLI's own exit paths, producers for the `kernel_death` class (a child's own `failure` is the one producer of `red_check`), the plan journal's own `Disposition::RepossessionPending` (F3a owns the road from the host into the engine's journal; until then a repossessed worktree todo reaches the engine as a child it cannot vouch for, blocks on the user, and records its disposition through `fail` or `drop`), and a root's own token grant (no flag sets one, so the token refusal binds what a child drew for its grandchildren and never a root's first child). growth +1494: two stages measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded, of which this one is +1032 (the stack of eleven stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D215, stands at +13707 over main's `{0.259.0, 91027}`): 146 lines in yi-types against 130 estimated, 881 in yi-runtime against 440, of which 60 are the review pass that follows this row (the verdict phase, the engine's budget draw, the build reservation and the two tests that die with them), 2 in yi-acp, 3 in yi-loop, 1 in yi-console and yi-tui one line smaller, with 23 of Python against 30. The runtime overshoot is the journal-resume path, the pending state with its retry, the return accounting with unknown usage, the read-only `ChildFeed`, and the waiter refusal, none of which the estimate priced; weighed for deletion and taken: `retire` gained a `commit` hook instead of a second removal path, `settle_or_restore` and the commit refusal share one `restore`, `fold_event`, `preview` and `update` moved out of `subagent.rs` rather than being wrapped, the record's `status`, `queued` and `repossessing` fields collapsed into `exit` and one `Phase`, the lease timer reuses the probe loop's due-time set instead of a task of its own, the TUI's stop lost its direct-abort arm, and the lease tests in four test files share one `tests/support/family.rs` harness. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.274.0 | 2026-09-20 | Messages are envelopes with kinds, receipts, per-pair order and a durable inbox; a request awaits its reply (D214, extends D165; Closes #458). This is stage F2a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 7.1 to 7.3 and 11), with the child lifecycle audit's finding folded in: `rlm.send` without `followup=True` put the message on the follow-up queue of a child whose turn was over, which nothing drains, and the receipt said `queued`. `crates/types/src/mail.rs` is the wire: `Envelope`, `Kind`, `MailId`, `Delivery` and `Receipt`. `crates/runtime/src/mail.rs` holds the host's mail state (`Desk`: the id counter, the per-pair `seq` counters and the request waiters, under one lock held across a routing), the payload parser `Draft::from_payload`, the inbox write and the presented message; `mailbox.rs` keeps the one route and the one delivery and builds on them, and no second mailbox exists beside the first. The host fills the sender, recipient, id and order, and a payload naming them is ignored (`recursion_e2e::the_host_names_the_sender_and_a_kind_keeps_its_direction`). The envelope is appended to the receiver's session store before anything is delivered, for a live, finished or reaped child and for the parent, and is read with `history://<agent>/since/<seq>/custom/agent_message`; `custom/<type>` is a new closing segment of any `history://` listing, it pages as the listing does, a read does not grow it, and `self` names the reader (`recursion_e2e::a_message_to_a_finished_child_is_inboxed_and_readable_by_history`, `fetch_session::a_walk_of_this_sessions_own_history_ends_at_a_limit_of_one`). A receipt is `{target, id, state}` and the state is read off the delivery attempt: `queued`, `woken` or `inboxed` (`recursion_e2e::send_returns_queued_woken_or_inboxed`, `recursion_e2e::a_send_to_an_idle_child_is_woken_or_inboxed_never_queued`); `AgentSession::deliver` now answers whether it started the turn and `follow_up_message` whether one was running to drain it, each under the lock that settles it, so no word is a status read that raced the turn's end; `wake_idle_hook` queues before it spawns so two lifecycle notices to one turn keep their order (`recursion_e2e::two_messages_from_one_sender_drain_in_seq_order`). `agent_message.request` installs its waiter before the envelope can reach the respondent, resolves only on that respondent's `reply` to that id addressed back to the sender that asked, and retires the waiter once on a reply, a timeout, a refused send or a cancelled cell (`recursion_e2e::request_returns_the_matching_reply_and_times_out_without_one`, and live through a kernel in `recursion_e2e::rlm_run_round_trips_through_a_real_kernel`). A body over 16 KiB is refused with the alternative named and nothing trimmed (`recursion_e2e::a_body_over_sixteen_kib_is_refused_not_trimmed`). Python: `rlm.send` keeps its positional call and gains `reply_to`, `kind`, `ref`, `conversation` and `deadline_ms`; `rlm.request`; `yi.mail` with `send`, `request` and `inbox` (`test_yi_mail.py`). Behaviour an existing caller can see: a plain send to a child that is not running answers `inboxed` where it answered `queued`, a followup answers `queued` or `woken` where it answered `delivered`, and every receipt carries an `id`; the prompt fragments are untouched. Not built, and named in the plan: incarnations on the envelope (nothing respawns a name before F3c), the cancel flag in the child's loop and `failed` from a `failure` envelope (F2b), `progress` into `status().note`, progress coalescing, a bound on inbox growth, and the list of outstanding conversations after a restart; a message to the parent keeps the word `delivered`. The three receipt tests the plan filed under `mailbox::` live in `recursion_e2e`, where a faux child with a held-open turn already exists. growth +462: this stage alone, measured from the 0.273.0 baseline the stack's last ratchet recorded (the stack of ten stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D214, stands at +12675 over main), 77 lines in yi-types against 80 estimated and 385 in yi-runtime against 260 (the payload parser with its kind and direction checks, the waiter's retirement guard and the reaped-child inbox were not in the estimate); weighed for deletion and taken: `route` and `deliver_to_child` were rewritten in place rather than wrapped, `message_params` and the string envelope helpers are gone, `rlm.wait` and the request share one timeout reader, the presented transcript entry stands in for a separate `presented_at` record, and the three receipt tests reuse the `recursion_e2e` harness instead of a second faux child in `mailbox.rs`. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
