| 0.272.0 | 2026-09-20 | `fetch` pages: a capped read names the next offset (D213, extends D164; Closes #456). This is stage F1c of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11). The `fetch` host request takes `offset` and `limit`, and a paged reply carries `next_offset`, null at the end: bytes of a `local://` text, entries of a `history://` listing, chars of another member's `kernel://` repr, which is how a repr past the 8,192-char cap is read at all. A request that names neither key is answered key for key as before (`fetch_session::a_read_that_names_no_page_replies_exactly_as_it_did_before_paging` holds the reply's exact JSON), so no caller, tool description or prompt byte moves and the request budget is untouched. Edges, each decided and tested: a zero, negative, fractional or non-numeric `offset` or `limit` is refused at the host boundary and never clamped; a huge limit means the rest, except that a `kernel://` page is clamped to the read cell's `VARIABLE_MAX_CHARS` and names the next offset; an offset past the end is an empty last page with a null `next_offset`, so a loop on `next_offset` ends; a byte offset is the caller's arithmetic and can land inside a UTF-8 sequence, so both cuts move to a char boundary and the end always passes the offset (`an_offset_inside_a_character_still_serves_text_and_always_advances` tries every offset of a text with two, three and four byte characters at limits one to five: every page is text, every `next_offset` advances, and from zero the pages rebuild the file exactly). A page is refused where nothing stable counts it: on every other scheme, on one `history://` entry, and on `history://<agent>/tail/N`, a window anchored at the end of a growing listing, so any append slides it; the whole listing and `since/<seq>` are anchored at the start and page cleanly, and a paged read of the reading session's own history appends no fetch-log row (`FetchLog::remember`), so a walk of it ends at any limit instead of chasing the rows each page wrote (`fetch_session::a_walk_of_this_sessions_own_history_ends_at_a_limit_of_one`). The page's hash and its fetch-log row are the page's own; the log keeps one row per address and every reader of it is a membership test. `rlm.fetch(url, offset=, limit=)` returns `rlm.Page`, a `str` with `next_offset`, and sends only the keys it was given; a paged `kernel://` read is text, never the dilled object. `Resolver::fetch` is `fetch_page(url, None)`, `KernelVariables::read` and `KernelService::read_variable` take the page, and the reply is built by `Fetched::into_reply` so the unpaged shape is testable. Tests: `fetch_session::a_capped_read_names_the_next_offset_and_the_next_page_continues_it` (T1) and three edge tests beside it, the live-kernel page read inside `kernel_data_surface::a_live_kernel_fetches_its_own_variables_and_runs_bash_handles` (T2), and `test_rlm` for the payload. Reconciled against the plan: `roles.verify_quotes` still reads the whole page, because the digest it pins is the whole page's sha256 and a page's digest is not interchangeable with it in a stored quote; reading only the line's neighbourhood needs a digest rule of its own and is left as a follow-up the plan now names. Not built: paging for `tree://`, `family://`, `checkpoint://`, `plan://`, `agent://`, `user://` and `mcp://`, and a host-request journey through the real wiring (the handler is `Page::from_payload`, a refusal of a page named beside D164's `object`, `fetch_page` and `into_reply`). growth +12181: the stack of eight stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D213, of which this one adds 126 Rust `src` lines against 90 estimated (the trait and both resolvers had to carry the page, and the reply moved into `fetch/mod.rs` to be testable); weighed for deletion and taken: one `Page` serves all three units, the existing `VARIABLE_MAX_CHARS` slice in the read cell became the page's clamp rather than a second cap, and the live-kernel check rides an existing tier-2 journey. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.271.0 | 2026-09-20 | Two shapes ship as library schedulers under the engine's admission and step table: fork_join over isolated writers and readers, and scatter with readers bound to partitions and quotes verified against the archive; four more are specified with their prerequisites (D212, extends D211; Closes #455). This is stage F1b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 8.5, 8.6 and 11). `python/yi_runtime/src/yi/shapes.py` (stdlib only): both shapes are module-level `async def`s over `(plan, run)`, so the lease's identity check attaches a second `plan.run(shape=fork_join)` to the first, and their levers are module constants (`MAX_RESTARTS` 3, `RESTART_WINDOW` 600 s, `SCATTER_MAX_ROUNDS` 3). Each checks geometry before its first `start` and raises `Geometry(problems)` naming every problem at once: fork_join wants at least two todos that can run side by side, each a `Writer` in its own worktree or a `Reader`, and refuses a writer or an inline todo in the shared workspace; scatter wants two or more readers on disjoint partitions (a url inside another's directory counts as shared), each with a `schema` item so its answer is stored, and exactly one pending inline lead. One loop serves both: start what is ready in plan order, stop at a start refused for `admission` and ask again after the next settle with nothing behind it tried first, settle, repeat. fork_join restarts one_for_one: a todo that ended `failed` is retried alone, at most `MAX_RESTARTS` times within `RESTART_WINDOW` across the shape, after which nothing new starts and what runs is settled; a `retry` the engine refuses (`retries_exhausted`, `illegal_step`) is final and stays in `run.refusals`, and a blocked todo is never retried because only `failed` is. scatter runs rounds: the readers answer `shapes.ANSWER` (`{answer, quotes}`, a null answer abstains), an abstention, a failed reader and an answer with no surviving quote are dropped, and the lead, `async def lead(answers, number)` declared as the plan's one inline todo, sees only `{reader, answer, quotes}` and returns `{"commit": answer}` or `{"ask": question}`; a commit is submitted as the lead todo's product `{answer, rounds}` and goes through `done`, a later round declares `<reader>-r<n>` todos with the reader's own delegation and contract, the question as the note and the key alone as the label (a `TodoLabel` is eighty characters and holds no newline, and the lead writes the question, so any question a real lead would ask refused the round-two declaration until the review caught it; the fake host holds the label rule now), and the last round without a commit fails the lead with the cause. `roles.verify_quotes` fetches each cited url once through `rlm.fetch`, keeps a quote whose text is on its line inside the reader's own partition, and pins the sha256 it was read at; provenance, not entailment. The partition arm is what makes the disjointness geometry mean anything after the first start: the reader's wall is cooperative (section 7.6) and the owner fetches with its own reach, so a quote citing another partition, or a page outside the archive, would otherwise verify. The walkthrough fixtures are programs under `python/yi_runtime/tests/programs` (`typo-fix`, `arc-game`, `campaign`), and `plan_walkthrough::a_program_and_its_json_fixture_reach_the_same_plan_json` runs each as a cell of a real kernel beside its JSON twin on a second engine and compares every plan's state and version, its todos in order with their states, edges, attempts, retries, whether they are delegated and what a done one output, and the committed transitions in journal order, while `refusals`, `touched`, cause and note text, and a delegation's or contract's contents are not compared; `kernel_data_surface::both_shapes_schedule_under_the_real_admission_and_step_table` runs both shapes against the real `admit`, step table, verifier and resolver with a width of one, where the journal shows each refused start asked again and never overtaken, a reader whose quotes cite a wrong line and another partition is dropped before the lead, and a reader whose child failed is retried and dropped, the two legal steps from `failed` to `abandoned`. That journey found two faults the fake host could not: the host's schema subset has no union type, so `ANSWER` leaves `answer` untyped, and a bare string product is stored as text a `schema` item finds no JSON in, so the commit is an object. `Todo.decompose` looked a sibling edge up in the parent plan and raised on its own docstring example; it resolves edges among its batch now. Overhead on the fake host, in host requests: fork_join over two writers 9 against 4 sent by hand, scatter over two readers and a lead 18 against 2 direct fetches; every extra request is a read or a wait. Reconciled against the plan: the section 6.3 cap counts refused verdicts per attempt (`done.rs` `refused_verdicts`) and the scheduler sends one `done` per attempt, so the durable bound on a retrying shape is `RETRY_CAP`, and section 6.3 step 6, which read as though the lifetime `todo.refusals` counter were the cap, now says the per-attempt rule that `plan_ops::a_retry_opens_a_fresh_refusal_count_so_only_retry_cap_bounds_a_scheduler` pins; the lead is a function, not an object with `commit`; `verify_quotes` reads the line from the whole url because the host's fragment needs a tag, which F1c's paged `fetch` is what makes cheap on a large page; the Python test ids carry unittest's `test_` prefix; two controls were added (the real-engine journey and the counted overhead). Not built: a writer child as lead, `rest_for_one`, `one_for_all`, the four later shapes, and the paired T3 row against direct retrieval, which is user-run. growth +12055: the stack of seven stages, D192 to D195 and D210 to D212, of which this one adds no Rust `src` line (Python is outside the measure); weighed for deletion and taken: fork_join and scatter share one scheduling loop, the lead protocol is a return value rather than a class, and no restart strategy with a single caller got a type. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.270.0 | 2026-09-20 | Plans are programs: the `yi` library opens, attaches to or resumes a plan, declares idempotent todos with contracts, runs one scheduler under a lease, and records its cells as an audit artifact (D211, extends D166; Closes #454). This is stage F1a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (sections 8 and 11), the first user-space stage: F0 made the plan engine one host request with a journal, verified completion and worktree acceptance, and the kernel could drive it only through raw `rlm.plan_op` calls that answered in rendered text. Library (`python/yi_runtime/src/yi`, in the wheel the kernel already installs, stdlib only): `Plan.create(goal, request_id)` never adopts a plan by its text and a retried create is the same plan; `Plan.attach` reads a plan as it stands; `Plan.resume` reads durable state and the host's reconciliation findings, never saved source, and lists in `plan.unresolved` every running attempt nobody can vouch for, which the scheduler never retries, fails or restarts. `plan.todo(key, label, after, delegate, accept, run)` is idempotent by key: the same declaration writes nothing, a changed one raises `SpecDrift(key, diff)` and writes nothing, and the append carries the revision it compared against. `Todo` holds the ops (`start`, `submit`, `done` raising `Refused(verdict)` or `Stale`, `fail`, `block`, `unblock`, `retry`, `decompose`, `result`, which keeps waiting on a child that asked you something instead of raising, `cancel`); `contract`, `cmd`, `schema` and `example` build the yi-types contract with every criterion frozen by content when the todo is declared; `Writer` and `Reader` build the delegation and its wall. `plan.run(shape, budget, detach)` is one scheduler per plan under a lease the owner kernel holds: the same shape attaches, another raises `RunActive`, and `Run` ends `verified_success`, `accepted_by_user`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `unresolved` (a completion no contract verified is never reported as verified). The default shape starts what the host says is ready and retries an admission refusal after a slot frees; `Run.launch`, `Run.settle` and `Run.over` are the seam F1b's shapes drive. A child the host calls `stuck` is waited on, not failed, and one it cannot vouch for (`needs_you`, or a name `rlm.wait` no longer lists) blocks its todo on the user rather than leaving the scheduler to drop it; a finished child whose contract has a `schema` item has its answer stored as the product; a `done` refused by a verdict on the product fails the attempt with the verdict in the cause, while an abstention or an escalation leaves the attempt for a decision, since neither judged the product (section 6.3). Host: `Op::Program { cell_id, source_ref }` (`crates/runtime/src/plan/program.rs`) journals a cell's source, read back from the artifact store before the record names it, carries the sha256 `program.py` has once that cell is appended, appends `# --- cell <id> <iso-time>` and the source to `program.py` after the commit (the next record appends any cell a crash left out; nothing rewrites the file and nothing executes it), records a cell id once, and is legal on a done plan so a `retry` there can be recorded first. The library takes the cell text from IPython's `pre_run_cell` hook, which the kernel prelude installs by importing `yi`, and sends the record before the cell's first mutating op on each plan. `plan.op` now answers with the typed `plan` and `notices` beside the text, names the engine's refusal `kind` and a refused `done`'s `verdict`, and takes `artifacts` (`{media_type, text}` blobs, owner only, at most thirty-four of one MiB, stored under the store's own digest before the op applies), because until now only a test could put a criterion into a plan; `plan://<id>/artifacts/<sha256>` serves a stored blob, which is the host-minted address of an inline product. `rlm.plan_op` passes `artifacts` through, and the venv identity hashes every package under the wheel's `src`, not `rlm` alone, so an edit to `yi` rebuilds the venv (`crates/kernel/src/bootstrap.rs`). Gates: `help(yi)` is the documentation, `test_yi_help` checks every name in `yi.__all__` and every public method for a last-paragraph example whose names exist and whose awaits match each callee's kind, `ext_e2e::fragment_examples_name_real_kernel_apis` knows `yi.` and `plan.` and refuses a name that is gone, and `check_prompt_examples.py` derives its modules from `python/yi_runtime/src/*/__init__.py` and treats every `yi` class as a handle. No prompt bytes moved. Reconciled against the plan: a todo's key lives in its label (`key: label text`) because `TodoSpec` has no field for it and thirty-five construction sites; a builder's `local://` input is frozen at declaration, not at `start`; `programHash` is set on the `program` record alone; the scheduler lease is kernel-local until F2b puts leases in the store; the T0 source-record test is `plan_program::program_records_source_before_the_first_effect` because `store.rs` sits at its cap; the Python test ids carry unittest's `test_` prefix. Not built: a marker for a recorded cell that later raised, a token budget, and `shapes`, `mail`, `judge` and `recipes/`, which are F1b, F2a and F3a. growth +12055: the stack of six stages, D192 to D195, D210 and D211, of which this one is +346 (`plan/program.rs` at 181, the typed reply and the artifacts rider in `plan/request.rs` at 94, the artifact route in `fetch/schemes.rs`, and the op's arms); weighed for deletion and taken: the T0 test moved out of `src` into `tests/plan_program.rs`, a `key` field on `TodoSpec` and a second host request for artifacts were not added, and the library re-implements no admission, step or verdict rule. No new crate, env var, dependency or `AgentEvent` variant. |
| 0.269.0 | 2026-09-20 | A child's removal, abort and every other exit publish one terminal update; the TUI and console reconcile against the roster, survive bus lag and no longer hold finished cards behind a live cell (D210, extends D165; Closes #453). This is stage F1e of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11), a bug fix from the child lifecycle audit that lands on its own evidence. A child's lifecycle lived in the host's record map, the event bus and each client's cache with no shared transition: `SubagentHost::delete` and `SubagentHost::reap` took the record away without a publish, and `run_child` then found no record and skipped its own terminal update, so a TUI card frozen at `Running` was never committed and never dropped, and `rlm.delete_subagent` before a child's first event minted one such card per call. Host: `SubagentHost::retire` (`crates/runtime/src/mailbox.rs`) is now the one way a record leaves the roster; `delete` and `reap` both call it, so `reap` gains `delete`'s refusal of a worktree with no recorded disposition, and the accepting road of `done` marks the published branch retained so the reap it runs is not refused, and the final `ChildUpdate` is built from the removed record with a cut-short run forced to `Error` and the cause `interrupted`. `run_child` judges a run by its last assistant message, maps an aborted stop to the same cause, tells the parent `[subagent … interrupted]` instead of `finished`, and once the record is gone publishes, notifies and bills nothing, because `retire` already spoke. A run carries the interrupt epoch read when it was requested (`AgentSession::prompt_requested`, `abort_epoch`), so an abort fired before admission is no longer cleared and a child deleted before its first poll never starts. `spawn` reserves the name and the slot under the roster lock and builds the child outside it (`crates/runtime/src/subagent/build.rs`), so `states` and `list` answer during a slow build; the host's watch re-reads its counters from the session after a lagged bus. Clients: `App::sync_children` ends a running card the roster no longer lists as `gone` and commits it; both TUI forwarders are one `forward` (`crates/tui/src/app/bridge.rs`) that reports a lagged bus as a notice and keeps going, as the ACP forwarder does; a finished card waits only on the kernel cell it was born under, so a lost cell end cannot hold every card; a card adopted after the first tool event names that tool from the child's transcript instead of drawing `starting`, and the record's answer preview outranks a folded stream that lost events. The console row cache evicts its oldest finished row at thirty-two rather than dropping the new one, and gives no row to a child first heard of at its end. Kernel side: `RLMSpawnHandle.result` raises on a `stuck` child with its note, `rlm.delete_subagent` takes the handle `rlm.run` returns, and the wait it passes the host is clamped to the asked timeout (a float ulp past it failed `test_result_uses_wait_and_keeps_its_errors` about one run in six). Reconciled against the plan: `retire` publishes for every removal and `run_child` stays silent on a missing record, which is what makes a child's own exit exactly one update, a removal after that exit repeating it rather than contradicting it; the console cannot tell a retired child from a retained one without a protocol change, so its rule is the first-seen-finished one above; the commit gate keeps the born-under ordering the `a_child_that_finishes_inside_its_spawning_cell_lands_under_it` pair test defends. | 0.266.0 | 2026-09-13 | The plan store is an append-only journal with a typed JSON checkpoint and artifact blobs; recovery reconstructs without effects and reconciles the rest; hand edits are gone and a user's administrative ops are confirmed in the session (D193, amends D97 and D105; carries D26 and D53; Closes #424). This is stage F0b of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11, section 5 entire). `ops.jsonl` under `.yi/plans/<slug>/` is the commit point: one canonical, digest-chained record per applied or refused op, appended and `sync_data`ed under the store lease before the engine touches anything else; `plan.json` becomes the format-2 checkpoint (schema and domain validated on read, carrying the journal `seq` and digest it reflects), regenerated from the journal when it lags and refused as `ExternalEdit` on a mutation when it disagrees. `plan/journal.rs`, `state.rs` (the pure reducer), `recovery.rs`, `import.rs` and `artifact.rs` are new; `fold_user_edits`, `user_edits`, `HandEdit`, `running_by` and the frontmatter writer are deleted. `spawn_intent` and `spawn_result` bracket every `delegate.spawn`, so an intent with no result is listed as `NeedsReconciliation` and never re-spawned; `yi plan repair` reduces, regenerates and lists. `Op::Import` is explicit and lossless: the original bytes land as an artifact blob, body sections become notes up to 4 KiB and artifact references past it, and a format-2 checkpoint with no journal beside it (what a clone carries) imports the same way. `Actor::User` is minted in one place, `authority::confirmed`, by a process holding a tty asker; the daemon socket fans a worker's ask out to every attached client (section 3.7's verification failed), so the serve worker confirms nothing, `_yi/plan {action: submit}` and `yi rpc` apply the owner's ops and refuse an administrative one as `NoConfirmer`, and `yi plan fuse reset` or a `repair` with resolutions is refused until a tty surface submits it. The first review round fixed: the store's `.gitignore` and schema are published on the engine's first write (`lease` runs `ensure_dir`); a damaged first journal record is judged before the record count, so a read never hands back an unverified checkpoint; `plan.op` keeps only `bad_args` and `not_owner` refusals in its ring, keyed on the request id and the args digest, and the journal refuses a reused id with other args as `request_id_reused`; a malformed `request_id` is refused as `bad_args` rather than swapped for a minted one; engine-minted ids carry the clock; `init` and `import` consult the journal before refusing, so a retry after a crash replays; a `set` that opens a plan journals its `init` under `<requestId>/init`; an import over a plan the journal holds is refused even when `plan.json` was lost; a checkpoint with no journal is `JournalMissing` on read and mutation and blocks no `init`; a missing sub-plan is `Missing`, not `ExternalEdit`. Tests: the crash matrix in `plan_journal.rs` (injected write and sync failures, a real kill-9 child, the opening ops), `plan_recovery.rs` (no effects on repair, the ambiguous spawn, damage at record 1 and in the middle, the torn tail, the detached checkpoint, the missing sub-plan), `plan_import.rs` (every byte kept, a skipped release, the retry, the lost checkpoint), `plan_e2e::a_reused_request_id_is_refused_by_the_journal_and_a_bad_one_by_the_parser`, `plan_ops::a_user_op_is_recorded_with_its_user_citation`, `acp_protocol::a_submit_over_the_worker_cannot_confirm_an_administrative_op`, `plan_ops::agent_cli_cannot_reset_fuse_as_user`, `rpc_protocol::a_socket_client_cannot_confirm_an_administrative_op`. The second review round fixed: a reused `request_id` is refused across op kinds, not only across args (`plan_journal::a_reused_request_id_with_another_op_kind_is_refused`); a confirmed op binds the root the human saw; a failed first append removes the empty journal it created, so the store is not wedged (`plan_journal::a_failed_first_append_leaves_no_journal_behind`); an imported graph runs the domain validator before its genesis record (`plan_import::an_invalid_graph_is_refused_before_the_genesis_record`); the artifact directory is synced after the blob rename; `yi plan import <id>` and `yi plan import local://<path>` parse as the store prints them (`plan_ops::the_import_commands_the_store_prints_run_as_printed`); `load` reads the journal once. growth +3939: the journal, its reducer and recovery, explicit import with artifact blobs and the confirmed user path, measured for D193; the Markdown writer and the hand-edit fold were deleted for them, and the plan skill's Python went with F0a. Round 3 of the review: `admit` is the engine's own refusal on `start` (`PlanOpError::Admission` with the count, before any spawn intent; `admissible` is deleted and the held list is derived from the same refusals); the record cap is rehearsed before the first reap with an 8 KiB envelope for the reaped urls, so an over-cap `supersede` kills nothing; effect ids carry the clock like request ids and the reducer refuses a duplicate `spawn_intent`; the session engine's liveness is the host's hold, so `repair` lists only children the host no longer holds; the bare `plan://<id>` address reads through the journal and regenerates a lost checkpoint; the fuzz lane gains `Import`. Pinned by `plan_ops::a_ninth_delegated_start_is_refused_by_the_engine_with_the_count`, `plan_ops::a_record_over_the_cap_is_refused_before_any_reap`, `plan_recovery::a_duplicate_spawn_intent_is_refused_by_the_reducer`, `plan::dispatch::tests::a_held_child_is_alive_and_an_unknown_one_is_not`, `fetch::schemes::tests::a_format_two_plan_is_served_from_its_journal_not_its_checkpoint`. |
| 0.265.0 | 2026-09-13 | Plan ops are one host request over the engine the tool uses; a child's lifecycle notice wakes its parent through the idle-waiting hook; `rlm.wait` takes a cursor and returns states; the kernel-side plan skill that called four unregistered names is deleted (D192, extends D137 and D165; Closes #423). This is stage F0a of `docs/plans/2026-09-12-yi-operating-system.md` (section 11), which closes gaps 2 and 3 of that plan's section 1. Gap 3: plan mutations reached the engine only through the JSON tool, so a program running in the kernel could not move a todo, and the kernel-side skill that said it could called four names nothing registers. Gap 2: a child's terminal notice rode a hook that queues a steer and starts no turn, while doctrine tells the model to block on the child and end the turn, so an obedient parent sat idle until the user spoke, and the shared pending counter behind `wait` had the matching defect. `plan.op` (`crates/runtime/src/plan/request.rs`, new) takes `{request_id, plan?, expected_revision?, op, args}`, shares `parse_op` and `PlanEngine::apply` with the JSON plan tool, fixes the actor from the registry it is registered on (`Owner` on the root kernel, `Child(name)` on a child's, the way the child link overwrites `agent_message.send`), compares `expected_revision` on the plan the op names, and answers a duplicate `request_id` from a 64-reply cache until the F0b journal takes duplicates over. The child host's `notice` now wraps `wake_idle_hook`, so a parent that ended its turn blocked on a child starts the next turn when the child finishes, and a notice arriving at the parent's turn end is not lost. `rlm.wait(timeout, cursor=None)` returns `{cursor, changed, states, notes}` from a family epoch every spawn, report, finish, interrupt and delete moves, so two waiters never steal each other's updates, a late waiter sees the terminal state at once, and a delete wakes a waiter with the child off `states`; `updated` stays one release. `RLMSpawnHandle.result` awaits `wait` with its own cursor instead of polling `list_subagents`, keeps its 540 s default and missing-child error, and collects a child that ended asking its parent (`needs_you`) instead of timing out; `rlm.plan_op` is the kernel-side call. `python/skills/plan/` and its `PYTHON_SKILLS` entry are gone; `skills/yi/plan/` stays. | 0.264.0 | 2026-09-19 | Yi reads where it runs and tells the model (D209, #444). growth +793: five stages, of which this one is +169 — the probe and its vendor tables. | 0.262.0 | 2026-09-19 | "Always allow" keeps a directory or a subcommand, not one exact call (D207, #442). growth +434: three stages together, of which this one is +273 — the grant reader and its two identities in `yi_permission::rules`, and the option list each surface now builds instead of a constant. The narrower design was one always-option with no label, which is what shipped before and is what the incident is: a grant nobody can see the scope of. Deleting the static `OPTIONS` paid part of it back; `CallOutcome::identity` and the `denied` argument went with D206, which is where the memory that read them was replaced. A structured tool's session rule was keyed on its whole arguments JSON, patch body and all, so "Allow always" on an edit never matched the next edit and the user was asked again for every file; a command's rule was keyed on its exact text. The prompt now offers what the grant would cover: `Always allow edits under crates/tui/src`, `Always allow edits anywhere in this tree`, or ``Always allow `git worktree` in this tree``, and keeps the one chosen. A grant is bounded by the tree its label names: a command is grantable only when its program is named on PATH, not by path, and carries no `-C`, `--git-dir` or `--work-tree`, so `git -C /elsewhere worktree remove --force` is not covered by a granted `git worktree`; a write at the tree root offers the exact call first, so `a` never hands over the repository. A later path write is allowed when every target sits under a granted directory inside the working tree; a later command when its single unproven program and verb is granted, and never when a segment is destructive, reaches the network, carries an env-prefixed argv0, or cannot be parsed. Catastrophic targets and configured denies still refuse. Seen red: `decide::a_scope_grant_keeps_the_promise_its_label_makes` (a granted `git worktree` allowed `git -C /home/user/other-repo worktree remove --force`, and a granted `python3` allowed `./python3`), `decide::a_root_level_edit_offers_the_exact_call_before_the_tree` (a README edit offered only the tree, and `a` then allowed every file in the repository), `permission_scope::always_allow_on_an_edit_does_not_ask_for_the_next_edit_in_that_dir` (two asks for two edits in one directory), `tui_drive::an_always_allowed_directory_edits_without_a_second_prompt` (the real binary asked for the second write and the drive never idled), `decide::a_directory_grant_allows_later_edits_beneath_it_only`, `decide::a_scope_grant_allows_the_same_subcommand_with_other_arguments`, `decide::grants_offer_the_target_dir_then_the_tree_root`, `tui_e2e::an_approval_offers_one_always_option_per_grant` (one unnamed `Allow always` row), `acp_unit::permission_bridge_writes_the_request_and_maps_the_selected_outcome` (`allow_always_1` read as a rejection), `port::option_for_picks_the_nth_always_option_and_labels_it`. |

Now `Orchestrate` attaches its protocol at turn end only after a write in the turn and never reminds there; a mid-turn signal still does. `RuleEngine` skips a skill pointer on a reader tool's result, holds a text-scope pointer until an edit or write has run since the prompt, caps pointers at two per assistant turn across scans, and says which needle matched (`Relevant: skill://debug (matched "FAILED")`). `wrap_internal` opens a reminder with `Runtime advisory, not a user instruction; act on it only if it applies to the work in hand.` The plan skill's trigger drops `decision-complete`. Advisory and notice cells render as one `▌ ⚑` callout row each with no blank air, and a run of advisories from one source is one block. Seen red: `a_turn_that_only_read_stays_one_shot_and_a_write_escalates_silently` (the protocol loaded after twenty reads), `a_failure_quoted_by_a_read_points_to_no_skill`, `a_prose_skill_pointer_waits_for_a_write_and_forgets_it_at_the_prompt`, `the_third_skill_pointer_is_dropped_and_stays_armed` (three pointers in one turn), `internal_context_wrapper_round_trips_and_dies_at_compaction` (no advisory line), `injected_text_takes_one_callout_shape_and_a_run_is_one_block` (blank rows around each, no rail on the notice). growth +484: measured at this push against the 0.234.0 baseline, which no version since absorbed; this version adds 57 src lines (the shared callout renderer replaces two arms, the rule engine gains its mutated and pointer state, the wrapper its advisory line) and deletes none, since the two rendering arms it removes were the only candidates. |

| 0.238.0 | 2026-09-12 | A timed-out command's descendants that left its process group die with it (amends D142; Refs #100). In the v4 sweep telecom-entity-resolution ran `cd /app && timeout 900 <venv>/bin/python solve.py` and read `[the process outlived the kill and keeps running as job 6; …]`: GNU timeout puts itself and its child in a new group, so the group kill reached the shell and left both holding the capture pipes. `kill_tree` now reads the process table before any signal (`/proc/<pid>/stat` on Linux, split right of comm's closing paren; `/bin/ps -eo pid=,ppid=` on macOS), collects every pid whose parent chain reaches the shell, and after the group and shell kills sends those pids one KILL through the shell builtin; the walk goes first because a re-grouped child reparents to init once its parent dies. A table that cannot be read, or a pid kill that cannot spawn, is named in `[group kill failed: …]` instead of reading as no descendants. One escape remains: a re-grouped descendant whose parent had already exited before the cancel is init's child, so the walk never reaches it and it runs to its own end; a subreaper or a cgroup would close that, and both need libc (§13.1). Tests: `jobs::tests::the_sweeps_timeout_python_chain_dies_at_the_bash_timeout` (the trial's `cd … && timeout … python … && ls` chain through the bash timeout; unfixed, it reads the sweep's `outlived the kill` line) and `process::tests::a_grandchild_that_left_the_group_dies_with_the_tree` (a `preexec_fn=os.setpgrp` grandchild, python 3.2 and later; unfixed, it held stdout for 30 s); both want a `ready` line first, so a missing `python3` fails instead of skipping. growth +204: measured at the push under D142; this version adds about 85 src lines and 57 test lines, and the other 62 are 0.235.0 to 0.237.0 against the 0.234.0 baseline. |

| 0.229.0 | 2026-09-11 | A run of four or more `<` no longer re-forms the untrusted-content fence sentinel (amends D98; Refs #100). `str::replace` does not overlap and the escape `<\<<` ends in `<<`, so an AGENTS.md line opening `<<<<yi-external` came out as `<\<<<yi-external`, which still holds `<<<yi-external`: a task-authored instruction file could forge a `trust="granted"` header inside the yard. `escape_sentinel` in `ext/assemble.rs` repeats the escape until no `<<<` is left; each pass lowers the overlapping `<<<` count, so the loop is bounded by the text's length and ends within three passes (a run of seven reads `<\<\<<\<\<<`, a run of three still `<\<<`). The yard's `sanitize` and `fetch::fence_untrusted` both call it, so fetch keeps no copy of the escape. The yard still strips control characters after the escape; moving the strip first is a separate fix. | 0.219.0 | 2026-09-10 | A capped command stream keeps its end as well as its start (amends D161; Refs #100). `drain_capped` kept each stream's first 30,000 bytes and dropped the rest, so a `pytest -q` whose failures ran past that lost its `FAILED` lines and its `N failed, M passed` summary from the result, from D161's reduced view and from the file its `[full output: path]` pointer names, and a finished job's report showed the opposite end from its live polls. | 0.218.0 | 2026-09-10 | The repeat breaker counts a batch over the last six turns and sees a text-only turn (D179, amends D152; Refs #100). Each turn's batch, or the text of a turn with no calls collapsed to single spaces and cut at 256 characters, now joins a window of the last six; a third copy in the window sends the hidden steer once the last three turns each repeated a batch already in the window, and the sixth, which must fill the window, ends the run, so two batches taking turns are steered once and never ended. The steer goes once per stretch, until a batch new to the window shows progress: two batches taking turns hold the count at three, and a steer on every such turn would itself keep two replies re-driving without end. A fresh edit before each run of one check is new to the window, so that loop is never steered, and an identical batch draws the steer at its fourth run rather than its third. A follow-up prompt starts an empty window, as it starts its own cut count. A job poll and a cut turn, which keeps no text, still count for nothing, and the message no longer claims the same result, which was never compared. Replayed over 253 archived sessions the rule sends 11 steers in 4 sessions where D152 sent 6 in 3, none with an edit in the five turns before, and would have ended Lb45bGG at turn 215; a steer at any third copy sent 31 in 15, and four of them landed on an edit and a check taking turns, two of them in v4 trials. | 0.216.0 | 2026-09-10 | `--deadline` is enforced, not only shown (D177, amends D150; Refs #100). D150 put the clock in the environment block and nothing read it, so a command or a kernel cell that outlived the budget held its turn until harbor killed the container. The session now holds one `Deadline`, set once from the wiring, and three readers share it: the environment line; the tool cancel flag, which fires at zero the way Esc does, so bash's watchdog kills the process group and the kernel watcher interrupts the cell; and the loop's `should_stop_after_turn`, which ends the run after the turn in flight once 240 s, or a quarter of a shorter budget, is all that is left. The turn is never cut: it settles and `yi ask` exits 0. The margin is 240 s, not 120 s, because the longest request measured, a settled reasoning cut, ran about 170 s and one that starts inside the margin must finish before harbor's kill; a flat margin would be longer than the whole 120 s and 180 s budgets of six fixtures and end each of their runs after one turn. A child session starts its own clock from the same total, and a headless TUI drive's `--deadline` now bounds its session as well as its script. `yi ask` also read a lagged event stream as the end of the run and exited 0 mid-turn; it now prints the count to stderr and reads on. `run_serve_command` moves to shells.rs beside the other launchers, since main.rs sat at its 1,200-line ceiling. | 0.215.0 | 2026-09-10 | A kernel cell has a wall clock, and an abandoned snapshot no longer refuses the next cell (D176; Refs #100). The ipython watcher's only exit was the interrupt, so a `while True:` loop, a blocking read or a `%%bash` cell held the turn until harbor killed the container. A cell is now interrupted at `cell_ceiling` on the kernel service's options, bash's 600 s ceiling unless set, on a clock that starts after the kernel boots, and the tool's description says so. The debounced snapshot's five-second abort forced its cell to `Aborted` after the grace but kept it in the active slot, so the user's next cell waited out the busy window and ended `BusyAfterInterrupt`, which restarts the kernel with at most its last finished snapshot; Yi's own cells now give the slot up, and a user cell keeps it for the busy-reuse recovery. Clearing the slot alone lost the next cell instead: ipykernel aborts every request queued behind a failed one, and Yi read the aborted request as an empty success, so internal cells go out with `stop_on_error` off. A `%%bash` cell comes back aborted at the ceiling, but its shell runs on: IPython's magic ends the cell on a `TypeError` without stopping it (a probe's child wrote its file after the abort), and 0.214.0's group kill covers the bash tool, not the kernel. A child handle's `result` waited 900 s by default and the doctrine and orchestrate examples waited 900 s in one cell, which the ceiling now cuts at 600 s before the child's answer or the documented `TimeoutError` arrives; the default is 540 s and the examples, which wait 120 s first in the same cell, collect with `timeout=420`. | 0.214.0 | 2026-09-10 | A timed-out command's process group is killed without a `kill` binary (amends D142; Refs #100). `kill_tree` spawned `kill -KILL -- -<pgid>` through PATH and dropped the result; slim benchmark images ship no `kill(1)` (Debian's lives in `procps`), so the ENOENT went unseen and a timed-out `python3` outlived its shell and kept running as a job on three trials. The group kill now runs `/bin/sh -c 'kill -KILL "-$1"'`, the shell's builtin, in the one form dash and bash both parse (dash refuses `--` and `-s KILL`), and goes before the shell's own kill, since macOS answers EPERM for a group whose only member is a zombie, and not at all once the shell is reaped, when its pid may name another group; a kill that fails is named as `[group kill failed: …]` in the bash result, or in the job's late report when the surviving group held the pipes past the kill's five-second grace. The kernel's bootstrap lock and the plan store's lease probe their holder the same way, and a probe that cannot run no longer reads as a dead holder whose lock may be taken: `process_is_running` returns the error and `lock_is_stale` leaves the call to the lock's age, as for a lock without a pid. The lane warmer's stop probes and signals through the builtin too, and fails on a probe that cannot run instead of resetting the slot under a live install. | 0.213.0 | 2026-09-10 | An in-band provider error is retried and settled like a dropped stream (D175, amends D79, D146 and D159; Refs #100). An OpenRouter error chunk (`Internal Server Error (upstream Wafer, code 502, server_error)`) ends a stream that returns cleanly, with no usage and no transport token, so the loop's retry rule, which asks for generated tokens or a transport class, saw neither: the run ended on the first error, `yi ask --json` exited 0 with the hour unused, and the turn's usage stayed `unknown`. The mapper now marks such a turn's `rawStopReason` with one yi-types const the loop reads, so it sends D159's hidden `stream_retry`; the retry count returns to zero after any turn that did not end in error, so each streak of errors gets one retry instead of one per prompt, and two in a row still end the run; the completions path settles the turn an error chunk ended from the generation record (a stream that ends with no finish chunk at all still ends unsettled), as 0.206.0 does when the pump fails. A request span's class is read from the error message, the text only when there is none, so an `HTTP 429: …` stop reads `transport:http_429`, not `provider:error`. | 0.207.0 | 2026-09-10 | `read` converts office documents and PDFs through the kernel venv (D171; Closes #368). `firecrawl-anydoc` and `pdf-inspector` join the default extras, the venv directory's name hashes the extras beside the ready check so this venv and main's never rebuild each other, and a `YI_KERNEL_PYTHON` may lack the two wheels. A file whose head carries a zip, OLE2, `%PDF-` or `{\rtf` marker goes to the venv's python once, under a 256 MiB ceiling (1 GiB with `pages=`), a 30 s timeout and, on macOS, seatbelt with no network and the cache dir as its one writable root (a user and network namespace on Linux where `unshare` allows one); the converter sniffs the content, so a `.docx` named `.txt` converts. csv is read as text; Latin-1 and UTF-16 text is decoded with a note; an image is named as one; a NUL-bearing file that is no document is refused as binary. The Markdown goes to `~/.yi/converted/<stem>-<key>.<hash>.<kind>.md` (dir mode 0700, copy read-only), keyed by the source's path, length and byte hash and by the Markdown's own hash: a saved original misses, a forged copy is converted again, parallel first reads each stage under their own name, and copies older than 30 days or past 256 MiB in total are evicted. The copy is read through the ordinary window under the original's path (no path outside the working tree reaches the model), unclipped, with a heading outline when capped and a heading section for `find=`; `edit` and `write` refuse any existing binary document or NUL-bearing file and any path under the copy dir, while RTF and decoded text stay writable. PDF pages arrive under `[page N]` markers, `pages="3-5"` reads only those, and pages without a text layer are named and left out; only a PDF with no text page is refused. Spreadsheets lose empty rows and cell padding and gain a `[sheets: …]` line. A glob charges the Markdown, not the bytes on disk. OCR is pinned to `reject` and nothing reaches the hosted mode. The bootstrap records the wheel's formats and aliases as `documentFormats` beside a hash of the probe, refreshed at the next readiness check when the probe changes; `read`'s description lists exactly those, asked again on every request, or nothing when the venv recorded none; `bash` names `read` when a command reaches for `pdftotext`, `pandoc` or `textutil`. Round three, from playing Yi on two terminal-bench tasks and a coursework folder: a PDF page with a detected table or two columns arrives as plain text in reading order under a note that the conversion is lossy (a row stays whole; columns read left then right; headings restored), running headers are left out once, decks carry `[slide N]` markers, a one-page PDF carries no page marker, sheets lose empty columns and point a sheet past 500 rows at `pandas.read_excel` (`openpyxl` joins the extras), `grep` searches documents through their Markdown and names what it could not search, a glob converts only while it has room, a copy's first look is 12 KB with its outline first (shallowest levels, sampled, repeated titles once), `find=` folds typographic quotes, a copy adds no code refs, the kernel mutes pip's version notice, and the ipython description names `anydoc`, `pdf_inspector` and pandas. The math task's answer keys now parse 48/48 from `read`'s text alone (47 before), and the schedule question that came back with a neighbour's slot now comes back right. Two dogfood runs over 141 real documents from this machine: 118 converted (cold median about 110 ms, 5.3 s at most on a 239 MB Latin-1 csv), 17 refused with a stated reason (8 PDFs with no text page, 1 past the ceiling, 6 of anydoc's resource or malformed refusals, 1 encrypted, 1 Word lock file), 2 decoded, 4 read as the text they were. The plan's phase gate (docs/plans/2026-09-09-anydoc-and-pdf-inspector.md §9) was waived by the owner, so all three phases ship here; §12 there records the departures. Tests: `documents::{a_docx_reads_as_markdown_through_the_read_window, the_copy_is_reused_until_the_original_changes, write_and_edit_refuse_a_converted_original, a_scanned_pdf_is_refused_with_its_reason, the_content_names_the_format_not_the_extension, a_binary_that_is_no_document_keeps_the_plain_error, an_rtf_file_converts_though_it_is_seven_bit_text, a_partly_scanned_pdf_reads_its_text_pages_and_names_the_rest, parallel_first_reads_of_one_document_all_succeed, a_file_past_the_ceiling_is_refused_before_the_converter_runs, a_hanging_converter_is_stopped_at_the_timeout_and_on_cancel, a_document_with_a_unicode_name_converts, a_document_whose_name_is_not_utf8_converts, a_tampered_copy_is_converted_again, write_refuses_an_unread_document_and_allows_text_formats, text_that_is_not_utf8_is_decoded_rather_than_refused, a_glob_reads_documents_whole_and_counts_their_markdown, markdown_gets_an_outline_when_capped_and_find_returns_its_section, a_spreadsheet_loses_its_empty_rows_columns_and_padding, a_large_sheet_points_at_a_pandas_route_that_works, a_pdf_table_and_columns_arrive_as_reading_order_text_under_a_note, running_headers_are_left_out_once_and_body_lines_kept, a_one_page_pdf_carries_no_page_marker, a_deck_reads_slide_by_slide_with_its_notes, a_long_document_opens_with_its_outline_and_a_short_first_look, find_matches_typographic_quotes_and_adds_no_code_refs, grep_searches_documents_through_their_markdown_and_never_rewrites_them, a_glob_lists_what_it_does_not_convert_and_why}`, `prompt_drift::read_names_exactly_the_formats_the_installed_wheel_converts`, yi-tools `documents::{the_description_claims_only_the_recorded_formats, an_unbuilt_venv_is_named_beside_the_plain_error, the_description_follows_a_venv_built_mid_session, bash_points_a_habitual_converter_at_read}`, `bootstrap::tests::{the_default_venv_is_keyed_by_the_ready_check, the_converter_reads_back_the_formats_its_venv_recorded, a_kernel_python_may_lack_the_converter_wheels}`. growth +14511: measured at the push under D171; this version adds about 1520 src lines, the converter, its refusals, the copy cache and the Markdown A cut is a length stop by definition: `ChunkMapper::cut` marks the finish and the stop reason before the settle and the finish, so the `Done` carries the record's usage and the id. | 0.190.0 | 2026-09-08 | OpenRouter routes by its deprioritisers, not a throughput sort (amends D159; Closes #287, Refs #100). Row 0023 paid $1.21 against row 0021's $0.45 for the same 3/18 passes: `sort: throughput` disables OpenRouter's price weighting and Auto Exacto and chose upstreams at about twice the catalog price, while the slow upstream it was meant to avoid is what the docs' own deprioritisers handle. The default `provider` object is now `{"preferred_min_throughput": {"p50": 20}, "preferred_max_latency": {"p50": 10}}` with no `sort`: an endpoint under twenty tokens a second or over ten seconds of p50 latency goes to the back of the list rather than being excluded, Novita and Z.AI stay eligible at catalog price, and a `routing` object in the user config still replaces the whole object verbatim (`{}` sends none). Test: `openrouter::openrouter_requests_deprioritise_slow_upstreams_unless_the_config_says_otherwise`. | 0.175.0 | 2026-09-06 | Where the work is, in two verbs (D143, D144, docs/plans/2026-09-06-branch-visibility.md, Closes #257). One HEAD reader (`lane::head`, a bounded file read returning `Head::{Branch, Detached}`) replaces the status row's hand parse, the environment block's `git status --branch` parse and the pool's `symbolic-ref` spawn per slot. `yi lanes` prints, per slot, the path, the holder (with the daemon ledger's first-prompt name and root when the id is there), the branch, `modified`/`untracked`/`ahead`/`behind` against the last-fetched `origin/main`, the branch's age from its reflog, and for a left slot what a reap would lose; every read shares one 3 s deadline and a read that misses it says `tree unread` rather than `clean`. The status row reads `yi ⎇ lane 1` on a lane; the quit line is slot, branch, path and pull request. The verbs shrink to `/land "Title"` (merges `origin/main` in first, aborts on conflict and names the files, pushes nothing then; pushes, opens, and on green merges), `/land` alone (reports, finding an open pull request by head on the forge so a resume never opens a second), `/discard`, and `/lanes` from a lane or the trunk; `/pr` and `/base` answer with a pointer. A full pool at an interactive start lists what was left and asks, outside the pool lock, applying the answer only if the slot still names that session; an unread tree is never offered as lossless. The ACP worker claims under its store id, so a console session's lane resumes and two sessions on one worker hold two branches (seen red: `checkout -B` refused the second under the pid name). One poller per handle; the forge's job list is cut to 32 at the parser; the TUI shows `· 3 h ago` on a landing older than two poll periods; the model's environment block loses `land with /land` and gains `landing: …` while a pull request is open; the solo TUI routes the lane verbs off the render thread, which it never routed at all before; the compact view says `earlier attempt:` for a rewind summary, so `branch` means git in the model's context. `Pool::open` no longer creates the pool directory. growth +9263: measured since the 0.142.0 baseline under D143 and D144, of which this branch adds 533: the `Holder`/`TreeState` shape and the lane line, the full-pool prompt, the base step, the reattach and the off-thread slash route, less the three HEAD parsers and `/base`. Weighed and left out: a `Blocked` landing variant for a conflicting base (a steer carries the file list; a wire variant for a one-turn state is a §19 change), an action-ledger row for the prompt's reap (D81's ledger is the broker's, which does not exist before the claim; the prompt's line on stderr is the receipt), a `land` tool for the model (nothing lands a goal today), and the ledger join inside `/lanes` (the runtime cannot reach the daemon's file; the CLI joins it). |

| 0.148.0 | 2026-09-04 | A root session is worktree-first: it claims a lane and lands through a pull request (D119–D124, Closes #197). The trunk checkout was every session's working directory, so one session's `git reset` ate a sibling's uncommitted edits and the person's editor saw agent churn; a `git worktree add` per child was a cold build cache every time, because cargo, tsc and uv key their caches by absolute path. Now `yi` in a repository claims one of N fixed slots under `~/.yi/lanes/<repo-hash>/<n>` (`lanes.slots`, default 3), resets it to `origin/main`, branches `yi/<session>` and runs every tool, extension, plan and checkpoint there while the session file stays keyed by the trunk cwd; `--here` and `lanes.enabled: false` opt out, a cwd outside a repository never claims, and a claim that fails fails the start rather than falling back to the trunk. Git is the registry (D120): a kernel-released flock per slot says who is live, `<n>.json` records base, lockfile hash, holder and warm receipt, an orphan is a state file naming a session under a free flock, `yi lanes` lists and `yi lanes reap <n>` frees one keeping any branch `main` lacks, and a resumed session reclaims its own slot with its branch intact. The hand-back is by move (D121): `merge_into`, `discard` and `release` consume the lane; `isolation='worktree'` children claim from the same pool off the parent's HEAD and merge back into the parent's checkout, never the trunk. `/land "Title"` pushes and opens through `tea` or `gh` by origin URL, or through `lanes.land` (yi's own tree names `just land`), in the background (D122); the gate rides `AgentEvent::LandingState` (D123) — a 60-second poll, job names cut to 64 characters before the latch and the prompt, one steer per red job — shown as a status segment (`PR #191 ●●⟳ · main +4`), a HUD row, and `_yi/landing` on ACP, with `/lanes`, `/pr`, `/base`, `/discard` beside `/land`. A released slot warms itself (D124): the toolchain table's offline command runs Seatbelt-contained at `nice 19`, only for a lockfile hash a session already synced, receipted with pid and exit; a claim on a warming slot sends TERM and waits 30 s. `runtime::worktree` is absorbed into `runtime::lane` and the B11 feature row into the lanes row. growth +4029: measured since the 0.142.0 baseline under D119, after merging main (whose own D117/D118 landings added 1647 since this branch started), of which this branch's own work adds 1729 (the lane pool, the toolchain table, the forge adapter and landing state, the `yi lanes` verb, less the deleted `worktree.rs`). Weighed for deletion and removed: a `Lane<Held>`/`Lane<Settled>` typestate (consuming methods give the same guarantee with one type), a `compile_fail` doctest (the compiler is the test), a `Forge` trait (two functions behind one `match`), a per-slot `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (an in-tree `target/` in a fixed slot path is warm by itself), a second registry file (git's worktree list plus the flock is enough), and a daemon-owned fetch (a claim fetches when `FETCH_HEAD` is older than 60 s). Seen red: the journey with the claim neutered, the warmer test with the `seen.json` gate deleted, the child-lane test with the trunk as the merge target. |
| 0.147.0 | 2026-09-04 | The tool surface from the model's seat (D117, Closes #189; docs/plans/2026-09-04-tool-ergonomics-v3.md). Edit anchors never go stale: numbers cited from an older read, grep or edit output move onto the current file through the snapshot diff, a cited line that changed rejects with the current text, a block op rebases only when its whole old body mapped, the header tag is optional (`[path]` is the version last shown), and the prompt's re-ground rule is deleted; Python and YAML get an indentation block resolver. A bash view of one file (`cat`/`head`/`tail`/`sed`/`rg`/`grep`, no pipe or redirect) is tagged so it anchors an edit, and read-only commands (grid's query verbs included) are Read-kind calls that overlap, through a per-call `Tool::kind_for`. `read` answers for a directory (listing plus skeletons) and a glob (whole files while the budget lasts, skeletons after), `find=` shows the block around a match plus every reference to it, and a capped read ends with the file's skeleton; the `glob` tool is gone. `grep` is regex by default with `literal`, array patterns, `block`, `def`, `count`, `multiline`, and `replace`/`apply` (diff preview, write on apply, 50 files / 500 hits, every file tagged, Write-kind only when applying). `plan op=set` takes a markdown checklist with nested children as the whole list; the plan tree, the HUD (`Plan done/total · now: label`) and every plan reply show progress. An edit to a `.rs`/`.py` file carries `[grid check --quick]` findings or a named absence. Every cap these tools set names itself at the cut — kept over total, the cap, the next call — under the new loud-caps law (`.ruler/045-loud-caps.md`), which also made four older silent cuts speak (grep's tag cap, context clamp and binary skips; `get_context`'s per-file heads). growth +2300: measured since the 0.142.0 baseline after merging main, under D117, of which this row's own share is 1457 (main's D118 and D116 landings carried 745 and 653 of the rest) — the rebase module and the read/grep arms are the bulk, the checklist parser and the set op the rest; weighed for deletion and removed: the glob tool, the `regex` flag, the re-ground prompt rule, the `Missing hashline snapshot tag` path. |
| 0.146.0 | 2026-09-04 | The sidebar holds still, hides nothing, and the daemon remembers (Closes #192, D118). A row's activity is its last state transition — the daemon stamps `lastEventMs` when a worker says `running`, `idle` or `requires_action`, never per streamed chunk, so two sessions working at once no longer leapfrog on every list; and the console stamps the same moment itself when the transition streams in, so a session that starts a turn moves up as it starts, not at the next `session/list`. Rows sit under their workspace — the console's own root first, then each root in the order it was seen, newest first inside each — so the full sidebar's root headers no longer repeat as recency interleaves worktrees; the rail slot is the row's place in that order. The rail lists every session, windowed to the viewport around the cursor the way the full sidebar already was — the twelve-newest cap from 0.137.0 dropped sessions off the rail whenever the order moved, which read as chats vanishing. ←/→ in the sidebar still walks the workspace filter, and now says so in the note row. The daemon's session ledger is a file beside the socket, rewritten on every transition and read back at boot, so the other worktrees' sessions, their names and their unseen counts survive a restart; a stored `running` reloads as idle because no worker outlived the daemon. growth +745: measured since the 0.142.0 baseline after merging main, of which this row adds 92 — the ledger shape, its load and rename-write, the transition stamps and the root-ranked sort; weighed and removed: the rail's twelve-row cap and its constant. |

| 0.144.0 | 2026-09-04 | Compaction gains its recall half (D115, Closes #158, Refs #168). The window after compact held LLM prose plus the P17 floor and could not *discover* a buried entry id except by dumping `history://` wholesale — the JSONL already kept everything, so the gap was discovery, not retention. The window now opens with a deterministic extractive view: `<yi_compact_view>` carries this round's outstanding errors and a rolling 120-line brief that cites every summarized turn as `(#entryId)`, a successful tool result as a pointer (`tool: name → N chars`) and a read a later message edits as `stale`; lines that roll past the cap collapse into `[Earlier]` as `(#first..#last)` (cap 24) and a `[Kernel]` line carries the persist note. Only `ToolResult.is_error` and a non-zero `BashExecution.exit_code` make an outstanding line — the word "error" in a passing test's output does not. `compose_summary` composes the view **ahead of** the full prefix-aligned P7 and persists its brief and `[Earlier]` lines as data in `CompactionDetails.extra`, which is what the next round rolls: the view is never parsed back out of its own rendered text, and a compaction's retained tail carries no entry id at all rather than citing the compaction it predates. `SessionStore::grep` is the store-native half — case-insensitive substring over `AgentMessage::plain_text` and summaries, thinking excluded, one-line snippets centred on the needle — exposed to the kernel as the host verb `history.grep` and the skill call `await compact.recall("needle")`; the hit's `entryId` then pulls the full entry through the existing `history://` seam, and the post-compaction affordance names both verbs. A `gap: once` rule re-arms after compaction: the L4 wrapper is dropped with the window while `FireState.fired_once` used to survive, so the same needle never fired again. P7's prompts are untouched, no tool joins the closed registry, and old compaction entries without a view still project. growth +486: measured since the 0.142.0 baseline under D115; this branch alone is +530 against main (the view, its two extra keys and the store grep) and the difference is the 0.143.0 D114 landing — the view, its two extra keys and the store grep. Weighed for deletion and removed: the `[Dropped]` identifier audit and its eval (an ident the view already cites needs no second list), the `compaction` user-config key (nothing but a test wanted it), a fold mode and a keyword `[Pinned]` section (both duplicated P8 and P17), and a regex query mode (substring first; regex when grep measurably fails, the same bar as embeddings). |

| 0.126.0 | 2026-09-02 | The shadow checkpoint index is locked per gitdir instead of per handle. `Checkpoints::open` returns a handle, and every path that wants one opens its own — the turn hooks, `/undo`, the fetch schemes — so the `Mutex` that was a field on the handle serialized a single opener against itself and nothing against the others. git treats `index.lock` as a hard failure rather than a wait, and the turn-end capture is deliberately started *after* the session reports idle, so a `/undo` typed the moment the answer lands opened its own handle onto the index that capture was still holding: undo answered `git add failed: fatal: Unable to create ... index.lock` and the drive gate read the outcome it was waiting for as a frame that never arrived (`plan_and_undo_answer_for_the_session_the_turn_ran_in`, red only inside the full suite). The lock now lives in a process-wide registry keyed by the gitdir path, so all four openers of one project's shadow queue behind each other and `git init`'s own template copy stops racing too; a handle is still the only way to reach it, and nothing about the on-disk layout moves. Diagnosed from the run's own frames rather than from the diff, per 080: the frame under the timed-out wait carried the git error verbatim, which is why no wait budget was touched — the message undo printed was permanently wrong, not late, and a larger budget would only have spent longer arriving at the same red. growth +394: measured against the 0.108.0 baseline, which nothing since has moved, so the number spans 0.109.0 through here; this version's own share is +10, the registry and the field it replaces. Weighed for deletion and refused: plumbing one shared `Checkpoints` down to every opener, which trades a race the callers cannot see for a lifetime every caller has to carry. | Renumbered from 0.120.0 on the merge with `origin/main`, which had spent 0.120.0 through 0.125.0.

Two gates, both starting at zero. `scripts/guardrails/check_orphans.py` fails a `pub` field declared in a struct under `crates/*/src` that no line under `crates/*/src` or `crates/*/tests` reads as `.name` or binds in a `{ name, }` pattern, and a file under `baselines/` whose name appears in no file under scripts/, evals/, skills/ or the justfile. `crates/types` is exempt because the wire reads it, as is any struct deriving `Serialize`/`Deserialize`; a test counts as a reader. Its `--selfcheck` is the refute pass the flag exists for: each scan is disabled in turn and the selfcheck must fail for that scan's own reason. What it found and this version deletes: `SourceBudgets.advisories` and `emergency_ceiling` with their `Default` entries, `SoftInterrupt.urgent`, `ApplyResult.block_resolutions` with the two `Vec::new()` that filled it, `BlockResolution.anchor_line` with its two writers, and `baselines/file_size_budget.json` — 0 bytes and no reader from the commit that added it at 0.97.0 on 2026-08-31 to this branch two days and 26 versions later, because `check_file_size.py` hardcodes its 1,200-line ceiling. There is no allow and no expect for the lint anywhere — a crate is clean and denied, or pending with an issue. The one `tui` site, `composer.rs`'s `rfind` index into a paste marker, is a `.get(..)` with `unwrap_or_default` and a `saturating_sub`. | 0.123.0 | 2026-09-02 | Scrollback order is antecedent-before-dependent, not arrival (D107, #128). A message's thought commits whole before any of its prose commits: `commit_prose` flushes the uncommitted thought first, and the `MessageEnd` remainder is a `commit_prose` call rather than a fifth writer carrying its own copy of the blank/gutter/retain sequence — four scrollback writers, one prose path. A task cell is held until the `ipython` cell it was born under commits, drawn finished in the live region until then. A host notice is a `Notice` cell that titles nothing, opens no turn and draws no `›` rail, with each of its lines on a row of its own; the window title takes 39 characters and an ellipsis instead of a bare cut. A failed kernel cell's streams are bounded head-5/tail-5 outside verbose, and the head's `↓` count includes the traceback. `StatusInput::threshold_pct`, written by one site and read by none, is deleted. The incident is the screenshot session `01a0648f`: a one-line thought with no blank line never reached a stable cut, waited for `MessageEnd` and landed under its answer; a child that finished fast landed above the `⊙ python · h = await rlm.run(…)` cell that spawned it; a 49-row asyncio traceback filled the screen in every mode while the head said `↑ 3 lines` and counted none of it; and the window title became `Yi — [subagent … finished] Last answ`. Every fixture that had passed chose the shape where the two commit clocks agreed — a three-paragraph newline-terminated thought, a two-row traceback — so the pair tests carry the field shape: one unterminated paragraph, a child finishing inside its cell, forty-nine rows, a notice through `host_user`; and `tui_unit.rs` holds an exhaustive `antecedent` match over `Cell`, so a new family fails to compile until it names its pair test. Measured on the merged tree: `crates/tui/src` 10,823 of the 10,830 ceiling (it stood at 10,830; this branch is −6, the writer unification and the deletion paying for the rest, and 0.120.0's truncate rewrite −1), whole-tree src growth +83 priced from the 0.119.0 re-seed that landed with 0.120.0, test LOC 32,726 → 33,276 across this and the three landings below, dist binary 5,463,056 → 5,479,600 bytes. |
| 0.122.0 | 2026-09-02 | A schema is a well-formed object or it is refused before any answer is read, and a child that reported is no longer told it stayed silent (#125). `Schema::from_value` returns `Result` and walks the document once at construction: the root and every node under `properties.*` and `items` must be an object, `type` must be one of the seven names the checker knows — an array like `["string", "null"]` is refused rather than silently disabling the check, because `as_str` on it returned `None` — `required` an array of names, `properties` an object, `enum` an array, and the nesting bounded, each refusal naming its path as `$.properties.files: expected a schema object, found number`. Unknown keywords still ride to the model untouched and `{}` is still valid. `load` parses and then goes through `from_value` instead of wrapping blindly, so `yi ask --schema bad.json` exits 2 naming `$` where it used to run the model and stamp the answer as checked. The two seams behind it are closed as well: `check` on a non-object node returns an error instead of `Ok(())`, and an unrecognised `type` name no longer matches every value. The incident is that `h.result(30)` bound 30 to `schema`, and a `30` validated everything — the answer came back marked as schema-checked with nothing checked at all. `rlm.result` refuses the malformed schema as the caller's error before it reads the child's answer, and when a schema is given and the answer is prose the error now says so: "answered with text, not JSON, and a schema was given so JSON is required". The terminal notice stops contradicting the row under it: `ChildRecord.replied` is set at `deliver_to_parent`, the one child-to-parent seam, and read inside the same lock the terminal transition takes, so a child that messaged its parent finishes as `[subagent … finished]` and only a silent one carries `; it sent you no message` — the old wording said "completed without replying" directly above `Last answer: …`, and a test asserted the contradiction as the contract. `await rlm.run` on the bound method earns an affordance instead of a bare TypeError: `ipython_note` matches CPython's "can't be used in 'await' expression" and answers with `rlm.run is a method, not a coroutine`. The cassette `recorded-0002-await-method-and-result-schema` is the session that produced all three, replayed over faux: it locks the affordance on that cell, its error flag, and a ceiling of six ipython calls. `crates/runtime/tests/schema.rs` is new — two table-driven cases over nine malformed documents and one that proves a well-formed schema still carries `$schema`/`description` to the model. This version's own share is +86 src lines, priced from the 0.119.0 re-seed of `baselines/src_loc.json` that landed with 0.120.0, and inside the free band. |
| 0.121.0 | 2026-09-02 | F-B1/F-B4 (#126): `rlm`'s `result()` stops taking `schema` as a positional argument that anything can land in by accident. A real session called `h.result(30)` — `RLMSpawnHandle.result(self, schema=None, timeout=900.0, poll=0.5)` bound the child's intended timeout to `schema` instead, so the host received `schema=30` and refused the child's plain-text answer with `child "sub-…" did not answer with JSON:` even though no schema had ever been meant; the model read that as its own timeout, and reported one that never happened, because nothing between the call site and the host had ever looked at what `schema` actually held. `schema` is now keyword-only and type-checked on all three entry points — `RLMSpawnHandle.result`, the module-level `result`, `_RLMCallable.result` — so `h.result(30)` is a `TypeError` at the call, before a coroutine even exists to await. `RLMSpawnHandle.result`'s own poll loop had a second defect beside it: `deadline = 0.0` accumulated by `+= poll` and was compared against `timeout` as if both were the same clock, so the loop counted polls, not seconds, and a listing call slower than `poll` (or a host round trip under load) blew straight through the caller's wall-clock budget while still reporting a plain timeout — the same shape let a child already reaped by `rlm.delete_subagent` poll silently until the nominal deadline instead of failing on its first empty listing. The loop now reads `loop.time()` for the deadline and the loop condition both, and raises `RuntimeError` naming `delete_subagent` the moment `list_subagents()` stops listing the child, rather than waiting out a timeout that was never going to end differently. `python/yi_runtime/tests/test_rlm.py` is the package's first test file: 1,789 lines had none, because every test in the suite `just check` runs drove `SubagentHost::result` directly; only a tier-2 `#[ignore]` journey (`child_transcript.rs`) reached the shim at all. Six stdlib `unittest` cases run against faked `list_subagents`/`result`/`host_request` — no ipykernel, no event loop of the kernel's own — wired into `check_guardrails.sh` so `PYTHONPATH=python/yi_runtime/src "$PY" -m unittest discover -q -s python/yi_runtime/tests` rides `just check` under both `--fast` and the full gate. growth +0: every line is Python, one shell line and doc prose — no `crates/*/src` file is touched. |
| 0.120.0 | 2026-09-02 | The two byte-indexed `String::truncate` calls are gone, and the method is disallowed everywhere else so the class cannot come back. Both now cut on `chars().take(n)`, which cannot land mid-character no matter what the string holds. `clippy.toml` gains a `[[disallowed-methods]]` entry for `std::string::String::truncate` (`Vec::truncate` is untouched — the path is fully qualified), so `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` catches every future site the way it caught the five remaining ones, each already safe by construction but now clippy-clean too: `markdown.rs`'s blockquote and code-rail indent trims rewritten on `strip_suffix`, `ids.rs`'s ASCII slug cap rewritten on `chars().take`, and `orient.rs`'s already-safe walk back to a char boundary kept as `truncate` under one scoped `#[expect(clippy::disallowed_methods, ...)]` — the only annotation in the series, HAR's non-panicking forms preferred everywhere else. `check_panic.py` never saw this class: it regexes `.unwrap()`/`.expect(`/`panic!`, and a `truncate` call reads as ordinary code; the gate that catches it now judges behaviour, not spelling. | 0.117.0 | 2026-09-02 | Milestone due dates are measured from the forge's own issue history rather than written by hand into the tree. `scripts/forge_tracking.py --measure` sums the size-days closed in a trailing 14-day window — S=1, M=3, L=7, read off the `size:` labels the issues already carry — divides by the window for a rate, and writes each open milestone's date as `today + ceil(open size-days * window / closed size-days)`. The multiplication is deliberate: the division is done once, in integers, because the hygiene half of this design recomputes the same number and compares it, and a float that rounds differently on another host would be a red nobody could reproduce. Each milestone's description carries the rate, the window, the commit it was measured at and the formula with its own numbers filled in, so a date can be checked without rerunning anything; a milestone with no open issues is left alone rather than dated at today; `--dry-run` prints the table and writes nothing; and the script refuses to start without `FORGEJO_TOKEN`, because a measurement pass that silently reads nothing is worse than a failure. `.forgejo/workflows/tracking-hygiene.yml` re-measures weekly at 05:17 UTC Monday and on dispatch, with the repository-scoped workflow token — one file per repository, since that token cannot reach the other one. docs/forge-planning.md loses its hand-derived table of dates and its stacking rule: a table of dates in the tree is a second register that drifts the day after it is written, and the stacking encoded an ordering decision that belongs on the project board where moving it costs no commit. growth +384: measured against the 0.108.0 baseline, which nothing since has moved, so the number spans 0.109.0 through here; this version's own share is 0, no `crates/*/src` file being touched at all.
